# Lessons Learned

Chronological journal. `/reflect` appends per slice.

For curated, applies-at-every-slice rules see [build-checks.md](build-checks.md). This file is the raw stream; build-checks is the distillation.

---

## Slice 001 (diagnose-orchestration-fix) — 2026-05-09

### Worked

- **Voluntary Critic on a low-tier slice paid off concretely.** Critic-required was `false` per methodology (low risk-tier, no mandatory triggers). User invoked `/critique` anyway as a safety check. Result: 12 findings (2 blockers + 6 majors + 4 minors), all 12 ratified ACCEPTED-PENDING and resolved during build. B1 (nested fences) and B2 (impossible ID recompute) were real shippability blockers the design glossed over. ROI was clear.
- **Test-first discipline (TF-1 strict) ratcheted reliability up.** 18 test rows progressed PENDING → WRITTEN-FAILING → PASSING with no skips. The two write_pass.py fixture bugs (textwrap.dedent footgun + missing trailing newline) were caught by the failing tests, not by manual smoke.
- **End-to-end validation revealed a different class of bug than unit tests.** AC #6 surfaced two issues (contract over-reach + cwd-mismatch tool denial) that the unit test suite couldn't have caught. Worth budgeting at least one real-environment smoke per slice when the slice touches agent / subagent orchestration.
- **The 4-backtick fence parser handled prose preambles correctly.** All three Opus subagents in AC #6 wrote a paragraph of explanation before the fenced blocks. The greedy-match-on-opener parser ignored preamble correctly; the design's robustness was earned, not over-engineered.
- **Three small Phase-0 design-stage fixes (B1 / B2 / M5 / M6 wording) cut Phase-7 work substantially.** Locking the design before writing tests meant the test-first plan reflected the final ACs, not a moving target.

### Didn't work

- **The "Do NOT call Bash or python" contract phrase in SKILL.md + 11 pass templates is too strict.** Pass templates' Method sections require Bash for graphify queries. The contract contradicts itself. Discovered while constructing AC #6 subagent prompts; orchestrator had to override per-prompt with "no Write to produce output files; Bash/python ALLOWED for graphify within OUT". Slice-002 candidate to fix the wording uniformly.
- **Slice-001 didn't anticipate the cwd-mismatch tool-denial pattern.** The build was developed with TARGET = parent cwd (the AI SDLC repo); the validation was the first run with TARGET ≠ parent cwd. Subagents lost Read / Grep / Bash entirely. The orchestration itself is correct (degraded-pass artifact preserved); but the analysis quality is gone. Documented as risk R1.
- **Sonnet under tool denial gave up; Opus improvised.** 03a-dead-code (Sonnet) returned a "tools denied; please grant permission" message with no fenced blocks. The other three (Opus) produced contract-compliant output even with only Glob available. This affects COST-1.1 model routing — extraction-shaped passes routed to Sonnet may be especially fragile under environmental tool restrictions.
- **`textwrap.dedent` + f-string interpolation is a Python test-fixture footgun.** Three test fixtures initially failed because `dedent` doesn't strip outer indentation when interpolated content has lines at column 0 (the minimum-leading-whitespace algorithm finds 0 and does nothing). Fix: explicit string concatenation. Generic Python pattern; recorded for future test authors.
- **PowerShell `@"..."@` (double-quoted here-string) interprets backticks as escape characters.** The mid-slice manual smoke initially failed because the 4-backtick fences in the here-string were eaten down to 2-backticks. Fix: use single-quoted `@'...'@`. Generic Windows tooling lesson.

### Pattern

- **Critic strong on internal technical consistency; weak on environmental / runtime concerns.** 8 of 12 findings were within-slice technical issues (fence parsing, ID recompute, normalize scope, retry semantics, doc accuracy). The two real misses (cwd-mismatch + contract self-inconsistency across legacy templates) were both *outside* the slice's new scope — runtime / environmental conditions that only surface at end-to-end runtime. Consider adding a "Runtime / environment / cwd / permissions" dimension to the Critic prompt — `/critic-calibrate` candidate.
- **End-to-end smoke must happen before claiming validation.** Unit tests with fake subagent fixtures pass cleanly; real subagent fixtures behave differently in non-trivial ways (preambles, partial tool denial, model-specific resilience). The unit tests caught all the *helper* bugs; the smoke caught all the *contract / environment* bugs. Both are needed.
- **Subagent prompt construction is content-engineering, not just plumbing.** Embedding 11 templates × ~3 KB into 11 parallel subagent prompts is real bandwidth. The crib-sheet swap (m2) saved ~30 KB per /diagnose run. Worth doing similarly for any future skill that fans out to many subagents.

## Slice 002 (fix-diagnose-contract-and-cwd-mismatch) — 2026-05-09

### Worked

- **Voluntary Critic on a low-tier slice paid off, second confirmation.** Builder recommended skipping; user invoked anyway. Returned 8 findings — 1 hypothesis-correction win (M1: cwd-mismatch is one possible cause among multiple; surfaced GitHub #57037 as alternative) + 7 design-tightening wins. Pattern across slice-001 + slice-002: voluntary Critic on any slice touching cross-cutting concerns reliably catches real issues.
- **Pre-locking the canonical wording (M2) prevented a slice-001-style drift.** Slice-002's design originally offered two wording variants; Critic flagged the lack of selection. Pre-selecting + adding a byte-equality test (`test_pass_templates_match_skill_md_step5_contract`) means future contract updates fail loudly across all 12 sites if they drift.
- **Build-log "flight recorder" pattern keeps surfacing real value.** Build-log captured both the AC-collapse decision (5 → 3) AND the em-dash vs double-dash discovery — neither was in the original plan. Without per-event logging, those decisions disappear into commit history.
- **Triage-driven design-stage edits as Phase 0** worked again — build proceeds with already-aligned ACs/design instead of rewriting mid-build.

### Didn't work

- **5-AC structure was TF-1-incompatible.** AC #4 (meta-AC about prose pins) and AC #5 (deferred manual smoke) had no test-first rows. TF-1 strict refused at /build-slice pre-finish. Discovered late (Phase 6); collapsed mid-build. Lesson: **don't write meta-ACs** ("tests for above ACs exist" — circular under TF-1) or **deferred-explicitly ACs** (they belong in the verification plan, not as ACs).
- **RR-1 audit docstring is misleading about heading format.** Docstring shows `## R-NN -- <title>` (double-dash) as valid; regex `_RISK_HEADING_RE` only accepts single em-dash or single hyphen. Initial format conversion used `--` per the docstring; audit returned 0 risks silently; fixed by switching to em-dash. **Future slices adding risks: use em-dash, not double-dash.**

### Pattern

- **VAL-1 Layer B internal-imports finding is recurring (slice-001 + slice-002).** Both slices flagged `tests` (and slice-001 also `assemble`, slice-002 also `tools`) as "hallucinated imports" because Layer B reads only `[project.dependencies]`, not `[tool.setuptools] packages` or pytest's internal package conventions. Defer-with-rationale every slice is wasteful. **Slice-003 candidate**: `add-val-1-imports-allowlist` — read project's own declared packages OR accept `--imports-allowlist <path>` flag.
- **Critic miss-class accumulating: cross-cutting conformance.** N=2 across slices:
  - slice-001 missed: cwd-mismatch tool denial (environmental/runtime); contract self-inconsistency in legacy templates (tooling-conformance)
  - slice-002 missed: TF-1-incompatible AC structure (methodology-conformance); RR-1 docstring-vs-regex mismatch (tooling-conformance)
  - The existing 8 Critic dimensions don't map well to "does the slice's work conform to existing methodology audits, tooling docs vs implementation, and runtime environment assumptions?" One more slice with a similar miss → `/critic-calibrate` candidate to add a 9th dimension. Logged in slice-002 reflection.
- **Voluntary Critic ROI on low-tier slices: 2/2 paid off.** Even on this slice's narrow surface (text edits + format conversion + 6 tests), voluntary Critic returned 8 findings, all VALIDATED by build/validate. The cost (~20% extra tokens) is worth it whenever the slice touches cross-cutting concerns. Default heuristic for slice-003+: voluntary Critic if touching subagent / orchestration / contract-wording / vault-format / runtime-environment, even on low-tier slices.

## Slice 003 (add-val-1-imports-allowlist) — 2026-05-09

### Worked

- **Voluntary Critic on a low-tier slice paid off — third consecutive confirmation.** Tier=low, critic-required=false. User invoked /critique anyway. Returned 7 findings (0B + 2M + 5m); ALL 7 ratified ACCEPTED-FIXED before triage gate; ALL 7 confirmed VALIDATED at validate-time. M1 was a calibration-relevant catch: must-not-defer item without a test-first row, methodology-conformance miss-class — exactly the pattern slice-001 + slice-002 calibration awareness flagged. **Pattern hardens at N=3: voluntary Critic on cross-cutting low-tier slices is now a default, not a question.**
- **TF-1 strict catches methodology-conformance gaps when ACs are well-mapped.** The M1 fix (promoting must-not-defer item to AC #4 + adding test-first row #8) closed the gap that slice-002's reflection identified. The audit returned clean (8/8 PASSING) at pre-finish, and the prose-pin enforcement means future SKILL.md drift fails loudly instead of silently.
- **Lenient-API / strict-CLI asymmetry per ADR-002 was the right call.** The Python API silently skips empty-after-normalize entries (be liberal in what you accept programmatically); the CLI rejects empties via `parser.error` (be strict at user-facing boundaries). Both surfaces tested at the right level; no contradiction surfaced at validate-time.
- **The slice validates cleanly using its own ship.** VAL-1 self-application surfaced 1 Important finding on the new fixture file's fake `my_internal_pkg` import; resolution path was running /validate-slice with `--imports-allowlist tests --imports-allowlist my_internal_pkg` — the slice's own new flag. Pleasing self-referential symmetry; documents a valid resolution pattern for future fixture-shipping slices.
- **Real-CLI subprocess validation distinguishes "tests pass" from "feature works."** /validate-slice ran four ACs through both pytest assertions AND real `python -m tools.validate_slice_layers ...` invocations from project root. The cardinal AC #3 (slice-002 archive replay) was verified end-to-end via real subprocess, not just under pytest's import-cache.

### Didn't work

- **TF-1 false-PASS pitfall: argparse exit-code-2 collisions.** Asserting only `excinfo.value.code == 2` for an argparse `parser.error(...)` flow can produce a TF-1-invalid PENDING → WRITTEN-FAILING transition: argparse's "unrecognized arguments" branch ALSO exits 2 when the flag isn't yet implemented, so the test "passes" before production code exists. Surfaced at /build-slice T5; tightened the assertion to pin the canonical error message substring in stderr; PENDING → WRITTEN-FAILING → PASSING transitions are now genuine. **Generic Python test-first pattern**, not project-specific. Watch list for BC-1 promotion if it recurs in slice-004+.
- **AC #3 baseline shifted from 5 → 3 (positive surprise).** Mission brief's "vs 5 today" baseline was pre-fix. Post-fix without flag is 3 (only `tests` remaining; AC #1's setuptools-packages auto-read eliminates the 2 `tools` findings automatically). Net effect is positive — the implementation does MORE than AC #3 strictly required — but exposed a small mission-brief framing inaccuracy that future slices' mission briefs should anticipate when ACs are interrelated.
- **BC-1 trigger-keyword false-positive precision.** BC-PROJ-2 (LLM fence parsing) and BC-GLOBAL-1 (same) both fired on this slice because mission-brief / design contain the word "parse" in `tomllib`-parses / `ast`-parses contexts. Defer-with-rationale was the right disposition (semantic mismatch), but the substring-match-without-word-boundary-or-context behavior of `tools.build_checks_audit` produces noise. Candidate: `add-bc-1-keyword-precision` future slice.

### Pattern

- **Critic miss-class "cross-cutting conformance" reaches N=3 distinct slices.** slice-001 (environmental/runtime + tooling-conformance), slice-002 (methodology-conformance + tooling-conformance), slice-003 (testing-discipline-conformance + tooling-conformance). The existing 8 Critic dimensions don't cover this miss-class cleanly. **`/critic-calibrate` ≥3-distinct-slices threshold is now reached.** Specific proposal: add a 9th dimension covering: "Does the slice's work conform to existing methodology audits (TF-1, RR-1, WIRE-1, BC-1, NFR-1, VAL-1, TRI-1)? Does any documentation claim differ from the implementation it cites? Do natural-language artifacts contain trigger keywords for existing BC rules in unrelated semantic contexts? Will TF-1 PENDING → WRITTEN-FAILING transitions be genuine? Will the slice's runtime environment (cross-shell, cross-platform, cross-CWD) match the design's assumptions?" Recommend running `/critic-calibrate` after this slice archives.
- **Voluntary-Critic ROI on cross-cutting low-tier slices is N=3/3.** No more "should I run /critique?" deliberation for slices touching methodology tooling, skill prose, contract wording, subagent orchestration, runtime environment, or vault format. Default to running it; the cost is ~20% extra tokens; the benefit is consistently real findings.
- **Real-CLI subprocess validation reveals what pytest-only validation can hide.** Even when 8 unit/CLI tests pass under pytest, running the actual CLI as a separate subprocess from project root surfaces edge cases (the 5 → 3 baseline shift; the VAL-1 self-application meta-irony; the `--help` text formatting). Worth budgeting at least one real-subprocess invocation per AC for slices that ship CLI tools.
- **The slice can validate using its own ship is a powerful integrity check.** When a slice ships a methodology audit, having that audit refuse to run cleanly on the slice's own changed files is a smell; running it AND finding a known false positive AND resolving via the same audit's new feature is a pleasingly self-consistent loop. Future audit-shipping slices should explicitly attempt this as part of /validate-slice.

## Slice 004 (fix-rr1-audit-docstring-or-regex) — 2026-05-10

### Worked

- **Voluntary Critic on a low-tier slice paid off — fourth consecutive confirmation, with a fatal save.** Tier=low, critic-required=false. User invoked /critique anyway. Critic's B1 caught that the design's canonical example `## R-NN — title` would NOT match `_RISK_HEADING_RE` (regex requires `R-?\d+`; `NN` is letters). Empirically verified at /critique-time. Without the catch, /build-slice T5's first test run would have failed (test extracts the documented example; example doesn't match; test fails). **N=4/4 voluntary-Critic ROI; 2 of those 4 caught design-stage claims that would have failed at build time** (slice-003's TF-1 false-PASS pitfall warning + slice-004's B1 fatal catch).
- **TF-1 strict + regression-guard outside TF-1 (slice-003 m1 demotion pattern) worked end-to-end.** Slice-002 lessons-learned said "don't write meta-ACs"; slice-004's original AC #3 (R-1/R-2 invariant) was a meta-AC; Critic M1 caught it; demotion to verification-plan + must-not-defer track + regression-guard test outside TF-1 plan was clean. TF-1 audit accepted 3 rows; regression-guard test passed from creation; pre-finish gate clean.
- **B1 → R-1 fix landed once across 5 surfaces** (design.md §1 + §2 + §3 + ADR-003 Decision section + /build-slice implementation in 3 production files). All 5 surfaces use digit-bearing example consistently; tests verified each.
- **"Validate using your own ship" pattern reused — N=2 stable.** Slice-003 invented this (use slice's own `--imports-allowlist` flag at /validate-slice's VAL-1 self-application step); slice-004 reused it (same flag + same value `tests`). The pattern is now a stable practice, not a one-off. Future audit-shipping slices will inherit this convention.
- **3-surface coverage closed the slice-002 silent-zero-risks bug at all surfaces** (audit docstring + audit inline comment + user-facing risk-register.md L3 prelude prose). M2's catch closed the third surface that the Builder originally scoped out of the slice — a real under-engineering catch. The bug now requires a user to ignore documentation across THREE surfaces to recur.

### Didn't work

- **Python 3.12+ SyntaxWarning on docstring `\-` literal.** The original design.md §1 "After" had the prose mentioning `[—\-]` (regex character-class fragment) literally inside the docstring text. Committed to `tools/risk_register_audit.py` at /build-slice T5; Python 3.12+ raised `SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\-'` because `\-` is not a recognized escape sequence inside a triple-quoted docstring. Default test runs masked the warning; `-W error::SyntaxWarning` would have failed. Build-time deviation: rephrased the prose to drop the literal `\-` form. **Generic Python lesson** — applies to any slice that adds string-literal regex patterns or docstring prose mentioning regex syntax. Avoid literal `\-` (or any unrecognized escape) in docstring text; either escape with `\\-`, use a raw docstring `r"""..."""`, or rephrase.
- **The Critic missed the Python 3.12+ docstring-escape-sequence pitfall.** The Critic reviewed design.md §1 "After" containing `[—\-]` in docstring prose and didn't flag that `\-` would trigger a SyntaxWarning on Python 3.12+. Same calibration class as the cross-cutting-conformance pattern: **language-version conformance** is a sub-class the existing 8 Critic dimensions don't cover.

### Pattern

- **Cross-cutting-conformance Critic-miss class hardens to N=4 distinct slices, 8 sub-class hits.** slice-001 (environmental + tooling), slice-002 (methodology + tooling), slice-003 (testing-discipline + tooling), slice-004 (testing-discipline empirically caught + language-version-conformance missed). The 9th Critic dimension proposal in slice-003 reflection should now also include a **language-version conformance** sub-clause: "Will this slice's literal strings (docstrings, string literals, regex patterns expressed in prose) trigger SyntaxWarnings or errors on the target Python version (3.12+ stricter on escape sequences; 3.13+ may add more)?" Concrete evidence-base is now strongest in the project.
- **Empirical verification at design-time is a Critic discipline that the Builder should also adopt.** When a design.md claims an example matches a regex/parser/contract, run the example through that parser LITERALLY (a one-liner) before accepting the design. The Critic now does this; slice-004's B1 catch was the Critic running `re.compile(...).match(...)` against the design's literal example. Slice-001 had a similar miss-class shape (cwd-mismatch tool-denial assumption was unverified at design-time); slice-003 had argparse exit-code-2 collision (also unverified at design-time). N=3 across 4 slices; the Builder's /design-slice routine should add an "empirically verify all literal examples" step.
- **BC-1 trigger-keyword false-positive recurs at N=2.** Slice-003 + slice-004 both deferred BC-PROJ-2 + BC-GLOBAL-1 (LLM-fence-parsing rules) on "parse" keyword in TOML/regex contexts. Same defer-with-rationale pattern, same disposition. `add-bc-1-keyword-precision` slice candidate is now strongly justified; word-boundary or context-aware matching in `tools.build_checks_audit.py` is overdue.
- **`/critic-calibrate` is one slice away from runnable.** Skill prerequisite ≥5 archived slices; we're at 4. Slice-005 will trip the threshold. The proposed 9th Critic dimension now has 8 evidence points across 4 distinct slices — strongest evidence-base in the project. When `/critic-calibrate` runs at slice-005+, it will see this pattern with overwhelming clarity.

## Slice 005 (add-bc-1-keyword-precision) — 2026-05-10

### Worked

- **Voluntary Critic on a low-tier cross-cutting slice paid off — fifth confirmation.** Critic-required was `false`. User invoked /critique anyway. Returned 9 findings (2 blockers + 3 majors + 4 minors); ALL 9 ratified ACCEPTED-FIXED or ACCEPTED-PENDING; ALL 9 confirmed VALIDATED at validate-time. **3 of 5 (slice-003 m1 + slice-004 B1 + slice-005 B2) caught design-stage failures that would have failed at build time.** Critic ROI on cross-cutting low-tier slices is the strongest pattern in the project.
- **Empirical verification at design-time correctly ruled out Option 1 (word-boundary alone) before locking the design.** /design-slice ran `re.findall(rf"\b{re.escape(kw)}\b", text, re.IGNORECASE)` against slice-001 / slice-003 / slice-004 archive contents and saw that slice-004 had 3 distinct word-boundary matches (`parse`, `backtick`, `output`) — confirming Option 1 was insufficient and motivating the anchor-based Option 3. Cost: ~30 seconds; saved an entire failed-design cycle. **N=4 across slices for this discipline; treating as standard.**
- **TF-1 PENDING → WRITTEN-FAILING genuineness held under triage growth.** TF-1 plan grew from 4 rows (initial mission-brief) to 7 rows (after Critic M1 + M2 + M3 added migrated-anchors + anchor-not-in-keywords-violation + word-boundary-prose-pin tests). All 7 rows progressed PENDING → WRITTEN-FAILING → PASSING with distinguishable failure modes (`AttributeError` on missing field, `AssertionError` on rule firing, missing schema substring, missing violation kind). Slice-003's "fix not applied vs fix doesn't exist" discipline is N=3 stable now.
- **"Validate using your own ship" pattern reused (third time).** VAL-1 self-application with `--imports-allowlist tests` returned "Clean — both layers passed." Pattern is N=3 stable.
- **Schema-pin TWO-surface discipline (per Critic M3) caught the doc-refactor risk in advance.** Two prose-pin tests (`test_build_checks_schema_documents_trigger_anchors_field_name` + `test_build_checks_schema_documents_word_boundary_semantics`) lock both surfaces in both files. If a future doc refactor drops either substring from either file, the audit fails loudly.
- **Voluntary Critic surfaced a fatal AC #3 wording-mismatch (B2)** that empirical-verification-at-design-time missed because the design's empirical-verification table used a different sentence than the AC. Without B2, AC #3 would have passed via `\bllm\b` alone — the `\bfence\b` mismatch with `fenced` would have been opaque. **Lesson: design empirical-verification tables and AC examples MUST agree byte-for-byte; the Critic now habitually re-runs the verification on the literal AC text.**

### Didn't work

- **Empirical-verification-at-design-time covered anchor-match COUNTS but NOT algorithm-path interaction.** The design's empirical-verification table tabulated anchor matches per slice but didn't trace the `_rule_applies` algorithm's pre-existing `if rule.applies_to == ("always",): return True` short-circuit ahead of the anchor filter. BC-GLOBAL-1's `Applies to: always: true` made AC #1+#2 unachievable as locked — the rule short-circuited before the anchor filter could silence it. Caught only at /build-slice T5 when post-anchor-migration backtests still showed BC-GLOBAL-1 firing. Resolved as DEVIATION-1: BC-GLOBAL-1 `Applies to: always: true` → `Applies to: **`. **Generic methodology-discipline lesson — empirical verification should exercise algorithm paths (always-true → glob → keyword), not just isolated metric counts.**
- **AC #4 was authored as a numbered AC despite being a regression-guard invariant.** Same calibration class as slice-002 (5-AC TF-1-incompatible) and slice-004 (AC #3 invariant-assertion meta-AC). N=3 confirmation now. Caught at /build-slice T7 by TF-1 strict-pre-finish refusing with `ac-without-row`; demoted mid-build. **Aggregated-lesson candidate**: `/slice` and `/design-slice` should pre-flight regression-guard ACs out of the numbered list.
- **Pre-existing `audit_slice` heuristic bug surfaced** at /build-slice T4 mid-slice smoke. The default `slice_folder.parent.parent / "build-checks.md"` resolves wrong for archived slice paths (`archive/` violates the depth assumption). Workaround acceptable until slice-006+ fixes it; logged as a candidate.

### Pattern

- **Cross-cutting-conformance Critic-miss class hardens to N=5 distinct slices, 10 sub-class hits.** slice-001 (environmental + tooling), slice-002 (methodology + tooling), slice-003 (testing-discipline + tooling), slice-004 (testing-discipline caught + language-version missed), slice-005 (testing-discipline caught + **algorithm-path-conformance missed**). The 9th Critic dimension proposal now has the strongest evidence base in the project. New sub-clause added at slice-005: **algorithm-path-conformance** — "When this slice adds a new branch to existing logic, does each pre-existing branch correctly compose with the new branch in all the cases the design needs?". `/critic-calibrate` ≥5-slice threshold MET after slice-005 archives.
- **Voluntary Critic on cross-cutting low-tier slices is N=5/5 paid off, with 3 of 5 catching design-stage failures.** This is the strongest disciplined pattern in the project. Default heuristic continues; the question now is whether to make it default-on for cross-cutting tooling slices regardless of tier.
- **Out-of-repo edits (`~/.claude/...`) require explicit forensic capture in build-log.md.** Slice-005 captured the `~/.claude/build-checks.md` before/after diff because the slice's git diff alone is insufficient evidence. Future slices touching `~/.claude/...` should follow the pattern.
- **Empirical-verification-at-design-time discipline is now N=4 across slices** (slice-001 cwd-mismatch + slice-003 argparse-exit-2 + slice-004 R-NN-vs-regex + slice-005 word-boundary-alone-insufficient). The cost is ~30 seconds; the savings are entire failed-design cycles. Standard discipline now. Slice-005's lesson refines the discipline: also exercise algorithm paths, not just metric counts.

---

## Slice 006 (update-critic-with-cross-cutting-conformance-dimension) — 2026-05-10

### Worked

- **The 9th Critic dimension landed cleanly with the user-override-of-Meta-Critic audit trail intact.** ADR-005 + critic-calibration-log User-override entry document the override of the 2026-05-10 calibration run's decline. Per TRI-1 vocabulary the override is recorded as a user-override-of-meta-critique distinct from Critic-MISSED or Critic-FALSE-ALARM. The next /critic-calibrate run after slices 6-15 has a quantifiable success criterion (≤2 cross-cutting misses vs baseline 10) — first slice with such an effectiveness target.
- **Voluntary Critic on cross-cutting tooling slices is now N=6/6 paid off, with 4 of 6 catching design-stage failures that would have failed at build time** (added slice-006 B1 in-repo↔installed drift fatal catch). Without B1's catch, the slice would have shipped Dim 9 with dangling cross-references to in-repo content that didn't exist — exactly the cross-cutting-conformance defect class the slice exists to mitigate. Methodology pattern is now load-bearing.
- **Bidirectional out-of-repo sync sequence works** (slice-006 first occurrence; slice-005 was unidirectional). Phase 1 installed→in-repo back-sync produced byte-identical sha256 with installed pre-back-sync (B926CC66...). Phase 4 forward-sync of `agents/critique.md` produced byte-identical sha256 between in-repo and installed (AF6EE94D...). Build-log captured 4 sha256 checkpoints. Generic methodology-discipline lesson generalizes cleanly to bidirectional.
- **The 9-dim Critic strict-improves over the 8-dim Critic on cross-cutting conformance findings** — empirical N=1 (slice-006 Phase 4 M1 caught a slice-005 historical 8-dim miss). The dimension addition is functional, not just structural. Strong evidence that the user-override-of-Meta-Critic was epistemically correct.
- **All 11 Critic findings VALIDATED (B1, B2, M1-M5, m1-m4)**, 0 FALSE-ALARM, 0 OVERRIDE-MISJUDGED. Strongest single-slice Critic performance in the project. The 8-dim Critic at /critique time caught a B1 that would have shipped a Dim 9 cross-reference catastrophe.

### Didn't work

- **Design.md "Out-of-repo files touched" Phase 2 forward-sync table was incomplete on TWO mechanical-table-completeness counts** (DEVIATION-1 + DEVIATION-2 at /build-slice Phase 5). Listed `plugin.yaml` (which INST-1 puts on the do-not-copy list per `methodology-changelog.md` v0.20.0) AND missed `ai-sdlc-VERSION` (which IS one of INST-1's installed metadata files). Both surfaced empirically at install_audit reporting "methodology v0.20.0" instead of "v0.21.0". Resolved at build-time + corrected in design.md at /reflect. The 8-dim Critic missed both at /critique time — it walked Dim 7 (Drift from vault) and caught the in-repo↔installed content drift (B1) but did NOT walk through design.md's table cell-by-cell against INST-1's canonical inventory. **Calibration class for /critic-calibrate**: design.md tables vs in-house methodology rules' canonical lists — sub-class refinement candidate for Dim 9 at N=2 (slice-007+ if recurs).
- **BC-1 false positives on methodology-vocabulary slices recur** (BC-PROJ-1 + BC-GLOBAL-1 fired Important on slice-006; same pattern as slice-005). N=2 across slice-005 + slice-006. Both deferred-with-rationale per BC-1 v0.10.0 contract; defer disposition is correct (the rules' anchors fire on meta-discussion of `subagent`/`fan-out`/`fence`/`code-block`/`llm`, not on actual implementations). Promotion candidate at N=3 (refine BC-PROJ-1 + BC-GLOBAL-1 anchors with negative-context anchor).

### Pattern

- **Cross-cutting-conformance miss class is now N=6 distinct slices, 12 sub-class hits.** New slice-006 sub-class hit: design.md tables vs in-house methodology rules' canonical lists (DEVIATION-1 + DEVIATION-2). Other sub-clauses unchanged from slice-005 cumulative count. The new sub-class is the FIRST cross-cutting miss filed AFTER Dim 9 was added — it's not yet visible to Dim 9's body (the dimension was just landed). ### Worked

Without B2 catch, the slice-006 PMI-1 escape would have silently persisted into slice-007. **Methodology pattern is now load-bearing AND empirically improving** (catch rate at /critique time: 0% slices 1-5 with 8-dim Critic → 25% slice-006 with 8-dim Critic + CCC-1 sub-bullets → 60% slice-007 with 9-dim Critic).

Refinement of Dim 9's body needed at slice-008+ to keep target reachable.

AC #5 + TF-1 row + must-not-defer entry gate the closure; without the gate the escape would have silently persisted. Verified at /validate-slice: pre-slice `python -m tools.plugin_manifest_audit --root .` exited 1 with `version-mismatch`; post-build clean.

- **"Validate using your own ship" pattern N=5 stable** — slice-007's CAD-1 self-application on the slice's own end-state returns clean; VAL-1 self-application with `--imports-allowlist tests` returns 0 secrets + 0 hallucinated imports.

### Didn't work

- **Initial design.md "Out-of-repo files touched" table mis-named in-repo file as `ai-sdlc-VERSION`** — the file is `VERSION` in-repo; the `ai-sdlc-` prefix is added at install per `INSTALL.md:141`. Caught by Critic B1 at /critique. **Recurrence of slice-006 cross-cutting-conformance DEVIATION class at N=2** (slice-006 DEVIATION-1+2 vs INST-1 inventory + slice-007 B1 vs in-repo file naming). **Meets the "promote to Dim 9 sub-clause when N=2" threshold from slice-006 aggregated lessons.**
- **Slice-006 PMI-1 escape was live at slice-007 start** — slice-006's reflection.md "Vault updates made" claimed v0.21.0 was synced, but `plugin.yaml.version` was missed. The slice-006 8-dim Critic walked Dim 7 (Drift from vault) and caught the in-repo↔installed content drift (slice-006 B1) but did NOT walk through `methodology-changelog.md` v0.20.0's PMI-1 invariant vs the slice's own `## Vault updates made` claims. **Calibration class: methodology-audit conformance** (Dim 4 sub-bullet) — the bullet's specificity needs sharpening on "claim made in `## Vault updates made` MUST be verifiable by running the corresponding audit (PMI-1, INST-1, etc.)".
- **Em-dash `—` → cp1252 byte 0x97 on Windows console** (build-time DEVIATION-2). The CAD-1 audit's "clean" output initially used em-dash; subprocess.run with `text=True, encoding="utf-8"` couldn't decode the cp1252 byte; pytest emitted `PytestUnhandledThreadExceptionWarning`. Test passed (returncode captured separately) but warning is a flake-risk for CI. Replaced em-dash with regular hyphen at build time. Generic Python-on-Windows lesson; possible BC-1 / Dim 9 sub-clause promotion candidate at N=2.
- **Prose-pin negative-substring false-positive on legitimate context** (build-time DEVIATION-1). Initial regression-guard substring `"edit \`~/.claude/agents/critique.md\`"` (with backticks) false-positive matched the legitimate line-99 explanatory text "skill PRODUCES proposals. It does NOT edit `~/.claude/agents/critique.md` itself" — a CORRECT statement. Tightened to `"To apply, edit ~/.claude/agents/critique.md"` (the OLD action block's unique signature without backticks). Generic test-pin lesson at N=1; possible BC-1 / Dim 9 sub-clause promotion candidate at N=2.

### Pattern

Slice-006-15 effectiveness target (≤2 cross-cutting misses across slices 6-15) on track but accumulating; slice-007 alone accounts for 2 of the budgeted 2 — every miss in slices 8-15 reduces buffer.

## Slice 009 (refine-dim-9-with-design-md-tables-sub-clause) — 2026-05-12

### Worked

- **Voluntary Critic on cross-cutting tooling slice paid off at N=9/9 with 8/9 design-stage catches.** Critic returned 11 findings (2 blockers + 5 majors + 4 minors); 10 ACCEPTED-FIXED in-round + 1 ACCEPTED-PENDING M1 location-pin test. All 11 VALIDATED at /validate-slice. Fourth consecutive 100% Critic accuracy slice (running 36/36 across slices 6-9 — strongest streak in the project).
- **Critic finding paid off at build time, not just preventively.** M1 (substring-only test doesn't pin location) → ACCEPTED-PENDING location-pin test added at /build-slice → test CAUGHT DEVIATION-2 (`.find()`-collision algorithm-path-conformance sub-class) AT first mid-slice smoke run. The M1 test caught a sibling bug in the same slice that introduced the test. **Generic methodology lesson at N=1**: ACCEPTED-PENDING fixes that add tests aren't just preventive insurance; tests can catch errors in the SAME slice. Watch for slice-010+ recurrence.
- **Recursive self-application phenomenon at M2 — strongest single-instance methodology-internalization observation in the project.** Critic caught slice-009's own design.md prose committing the EXACT class of design-doc-vs-canonical-inventory drift the slice was encoding into the Critic prompt (conflated INSTALL.md Step 3f do-not-copy with `tools/install_audit.py:_CANONICAL_*` positive-inclusion). The slice authoring the methodology refinement demonstrated the defect class in its own draft. **Generic methodology lesson at N=1**: when authoring a methodology rule, the very draft is subject to the rule's discipline; the Critic should be EXPECTED to find rule-class violations in the rule's own prose. Watch for slice-010+ recurrence.
- **CCC-1 v1.1 ships preserving 5-sub-clause structural invariant** — body-level refinement of Dim 9 sub-clause 2 inline; `test_critique_dim_9_lists_five_sub_clauses` regression-guard PASS unchanged at every gate. Cross-reference structure (Dim 9 → Dim 1) preserved. Citation framing preserved. ADR-008 Option 3 (inline refinement) validated empirically.
- **N-surface schema-pin discipline (slice-008 M2 generalization) applied at slice-009 for the substantive canonical phrase `design.md mechanical tables`** — pinned across 3 surfaces (critique.md AC #1 test + in-repo methodology-changelog.md entry pin + installed methodology-changelog.md entry pin). The single canonical phrase shared across all surfaces makes drift impossible without breaking ≥2 tests simultaneously. Per Critic M3 ACCEPTED-FIXED.
- **PMI-1 versioned-gate supersession ratchets to N=2 supersession events stable** (slice-007 introduced → slice-008 first-superseded → slice-009 second-superseded). Per Critic M5 the framing corrected to "N=1 supersession event pre-slice-009 → N=2 post-completion" (slice-008's claim of "N=2 stable" was off-by-one). The pattern is now genuinely N=2 stable post-slice-009.
- **Bidirectional sha256 forensic capture ratchets to N=5 stable** (slice-005..009) — canonical practice now. Pattern is permanent methodology. Out-of-repo edits to `~/.claude/...` always paired with pre + post sha256 capture in build-log.md.
- **"Validate using your own ship" pattern ratchets to N=7 stable** (slice-003..009) — self-application BC-1 audit on slice-009's own mission-brief + design returns `applicable: []` (BC-PROJ-1 + BC-GLOBAL-1 silenced by negative anchors `aggregated lessons`, `meta-discussion`, `vocabulary`, `back-sync`, `forward-sync`, `Dim 9`). Closes noise loop. Pattern is permanent methodology.
- **Empirical-verification-at-design-time ratchets to N=8 stable** — slice-009 ran BC-1 self-application audit + test-pin substring conflict scan BEFORE locking ACs, confirming all 8 canonical literals absent pre-fix (genuine WRITTEN-FAILING transitions guaranteed). Cost ~1 min; saved entire failed-design cycle.

### Didn't work

- **Cross-cutting-conformance Dim 9 catch rate REGRESSED at slice-009** from slice-008's 100% to slice-009's 60% (3 caught at /critique + 2 missed surfaced at build = 3/5). The "monotonic strict improvement" claim from slice-008's reflection (line 215 above) is **broken** at slice-009. The 2 missed sub-class hits — DEVIATION-1 (case-sensitivity in canonical-literal pin crossing markdown bold + sentence-start formatting) + DEVIATION-2 (algorithm-path-conformance generalized to substring-search test impl) — are new sub-class candidates at N=1 each. Honest reflection acknowledging non-monotonic trajectory.
- **Cumulative cross-cutting miss-count target significantly exceeded** — user-override target was ≤2 misses across slices 6-15; cumulative through slice-009 is **8 misses** (already 4× target with 6 slices remaining in the window). The 9-dim Critic improved per-slice catch rates substantially but cumulative misses grew faster than projected. Calibration signal: `/critic-calibrate` at slice-012-015 should recalibrate the quantitative target OR refine the Critic prompt with slice-009's 2 new sub-class candidates.
- **TWO build-time DEVIATIONs in slice-009 vs slice-008's zero** — slice-008's "zero-deviation via strong /critique" lesson did NOT recur at slice-009. Both slice-009 DEVIATIONs were M1-class predicted (the M1 location-pin test caught DEVIATION-2 directly), so the failure mode quality is better than slices 1-5 (build-time fix at mid-slice smoke vs validate-time fix), but the literal "zero DEVIATIONs" claim only stands at slice-008 N=1.
- **design.md narrative inaccuracy (build-log NARRATIVE-1)** — design.md said "TF-1 plan grew 6 → 7 rows at /critique" but the actual count is 6 (AC #1 carries 2 sibling rows under the same AC number). Cosmetic, not a correctness issue, but minor drift in the slice's own narrative.
- **Initial Dim 9 sub-clause 2 body refinement had capitalized D (DEVIATION-1)** — markdown bold at sentence start naturally pulls toward capitalizing, but the canonical literal is lowercase. Build-time author error caught by AC #1 substring pin at first mid-slice smoke. Cost ~3 min to fix.
- **Initial M1 location-pin test had `.find()` collision (DEVIATION-2)** — naive `CRITIQUE.find("Algorithm-path-conformance")` returned the WRONG occurrence (Dim 4 body before Dim 9 sub-clause 3 title). Build-time test-impl bug; fixed by anchoring on Dim 9-unique cross-reference text. Cost ~2 min to fix. **This is the slice-005 algorithm-path-conformance lesson applied to substring-search test impl — a structural recurrence at the family level, which Critic should arguably have flagged.**

### Pattern

- **Critic catch-rate is RANGE-BOUND (60-100%), not monotonically improving.** Slice-009's regression from slice-008's 100% falsifies the monotonic-improvement claim at N=5. Adjust expectations for slice-010+: cross-cutting catch rate is plural; new sub-class candidates emerge per slice; the per-slice catch rate is range-bound at 60-100% on N=4 evidence (slice-006..009).
- **Recursive self-application is a real, recurring methodology phenomenon.** The slice that's authoring a methodology rule is itself subject to the rule's discipline at /critique time. Plan for it: when authoring a methodology refinement, the Critic SHOULD be expected to find rule-class violations in the slice's own prose. Promote to /critique skill prose at N=2.
- **Critic findings can pay off at build time, not just preventively.** M1's location-pin test caught DEVIATION-2 at first mid-slice smoke run — the test mandated by the Critic finding caught a sibling bug in the very build that introduced the test. ROI framing should include "build-time error detection via Critic-mandated tests" as a distinct category alongside "preventive against future drift".
- **Voluntary Critic on cross-cutting tooling slices is hard-load-bearing at N=9/9.** Promote to /slice default heuristic at slice-010+ — `critic-required: true` when slice scope includes "modifies in-house audit / agent prompt / methodology rule". The empirical case is the strongest in the project (9 of 9 cross-cutting tooling slices had Critic findings that paid off; 8 of 9 caught at design stage).
- **The 9-dim Critic improves catch rate but doesn't eliminate the miss class.** Cumulative cross-cutting misses grew from 8-dim baseline (10 across slices 1-5) to 9-dim (8 across slices 6-9). The miss class is plural and persistent; new sub-class candidates emerge per slice (slice-009 alone added 2: case-sensitivity sub-class + substring-search anchor-uniqueness sub-class). The dimension is doing its job at the catch-rate level but the long-tail of new sub-classes continues.

Terse bullet matches `External integrations (OAuth, payment gateways, third-party APIs)` shape; evidence-prose blockquote carries the longer justification clause + N=9/9 + slice-006/007/008/009 example anchors + sub-class anchors. Clean separation of concerns.
- **Slice-009 DEVIATION-1 + DEVIATION-2 pre-emption at slice-010 design time WORKED** — zero recurrence at slice-010 build time. Pre-AC-lock empirical audits (canonical-literal absence + location-pin anchor uniqueness) caught the trap-class before it could surface. Design-time discipline is load-bearing.
- **Critic at /critique stress-tested slice-010's own draft and found 3 rule-class violations in the slice's own prose** (B1 N=9/9 internal inconsistency + M1 bullet-style asymmetry + B5 rule-naming convention break). Recursive-self-application phenomenon at slice-009 M2 ratchets to N=2-cumulative at slice-010 design-time — exactly the prediction slice-009 reflection made.
- **All 11 Critic findings VALIDATED at /critique-disposition level** — fifth consecutive 100% Critic accuracy slice. Running 47/47 across slices 6-10. Strongest streak in the project.
- **Multi-modal evidence per AC (pytest + sha256 forensic + manual visual + audit replay + MCT-1 self-application check) is the right validation discipline for prose-edit slices** — no real device or user, but full coverage via pytest replay against actual edited files + forensic capture + audit replay. Shippability catalog regression check (80 PASS in 2.67s; well under 2-min target) caught zero regressions.

### Didn't work

None of the layers alone caught all of it; cumulative coverage was complete only post-/build-slice. Pattern: future cross-cutting tooling slices should explicitly expect design-semantic / runtime-prerequisite / environment-interaction defect-class lineage across the Critic-stack.

- **Slice-013 generic methodology lesson on shippability-catalog-consumer-reference-propagation paid off PROACTIVELY at slice-014.** Slice-013 caught N=1 reactively at /validate-slice; slice-014 caught N=2 proactively at /build-slice Phase 4 by applying the lesson. Row 13's pytest command referencing deleted `_at_0_28_0` was updated to `_invariant` BEFORE running the shippability catalog — catalog 14/14 PASS no regressions. **Cross-slice methodology-lesson propagation works**: a lesson captured at slice-013 reflection materially saved time at slice-014 build.

- **DR-1 dual-review catching pattern-blindness N=1 → N=2 stable post-slice-013 codification**. Slice-013 first instance caught body-bound widening defect class; slice-014 second instance caught imports-prerequisite class. Two structurally distinct catches in consecutive cross-cutting-tooling slices. Generic methodology pattern emerging: dual-review pays off most on cross-cutting tooling slices where the first Critic catches design-semantic issues but misses runtime-prerequisite or environment-interaction concerns.

- **EPGD-1 self-application empirically VALIDATED at slice-014's discipline-retirement.** 0 of 7 prior entry-pin functions touched through the PMI-1 gate Edit. Slice-014 IS canonical reference instance of EPGD-1 at the discipline-retirement level — the (a) ↔ (b) duality. RSAD-1 cumulative evidence N=5 → N=6 post-codification.

- **N-surface schema-pin 3-surface shape ratchets to N=3 instances stable.** RSAD-1 v0.26.0 + EPGD-1 v0.28.0 + PMI-1-v1.1 v0.29.0. Canonical phrase `version-agnostic PMI-1 cleanliness gate` pinned across ADR-013 + in-repo + installed changelog. Drift impossible without breaking ≥2 tests simultaneously. The 3-surface-pin pattern is now load-bearing across 3 consecutive cross-cutting tooling slices.

- **Empirical-verification-at-design-time discipline N=12 → N=13 stable.** Slice-014 ran 3 design-time audits (version-literal absence + pinned error-message substring presence + Edit narrow-scope verification) all VALIDATED at /build-slice. Cumulative cost ~1 min; saved a guaranteed failed-design cycle.

### Didn't work

- **/critique M2 + /critique-review M-add-1 fix was NECESSARY-BUT-INSUFFICIENT** for the regression-test monkeypatch concern. Both Critic-stack layers pinned the right CLASS (name-resolution + imports-prerequisite) but neither anticipated pytest's namespace-package import-mode interaction. The dotted-string-form `monkeypatch.setattr("tests.methodology.test_methodology_changelog.REPO_ROOT", ...)` silently no-ops because pytest places the running module under bare key `methodology.test_methodology_changelog`. Surfaced as DEVIATION-1 at /build-slice Phase 1d; diagnosed + corrected in-line (~10 min). Fix: object-form `monkeypatch.setattr(sys.modules[__name__], "REPO_ROOT", tmp_path)`. Same root-cause family as VAL-1 Layer B (N=12 cumulative recurrence).

- **TF-1 audit's first-integer-only parsing of multi-AC cells.** Consolidated cell `1 + 2 + 5` parsed as AC #1 only at slice-014's first /build-slice Step 6 strict-pre-finish run, leaving AC #5 with `ac-without-row` violation. m3's mission-brief consolidation was right but the audit-format constraint (one AC per row OR explicit row duplication) was tighter than the slice-013 EPGD-1 entry-pin shape suggested. Fixed in-line at /build-slice by splitting row 1 into separate AC #1 + AC #5 rows. Generic methodology lesson at N=1.

### Pattern

- **Methodology rules ride a "necessary-but-insufficient" failure mode** when the rule pins the right class but misses an environment-interaction dimension. Slice-014 M2 illustrates this — the name-resolution semantics class was pinned correctly (bare `REPO_ROOT` resolves from running module globals) but the pytest import-mode dimension was missed. Future cross-cutting tooling slices working with test-runtime tooling (monkeypatch, importlib, sys.modules) should treat **environment-interaction** as a distinct dimension to stress-test at /design-slice or /critique — alongside design-semantic + runtime-prerequisite. Generic methodology pattern: **Critic-stack layers should be expected to catch different defect-class dimensions; the cumulative stack should cover the cross-product**, not duplicate the same dimension at each layer.

- **Cross-slice methodology-lesson propagation has empirical evidence at slice-014.** The slice-013 reflection's generic methodology lesson (`shippability.md` consumer-reference propagation) was applied PROACTIVELY at slice-014 Phase 4 — saved a regression catch that would otherwise have surfaced at /validate-slice Step 5.5 (as it did at slice-013). The reflection record IS load-bearing across slices when read at /build-slice plan time. Future slices doing cross-cutting tooling work should scan recent reflections' "Lessons for next slice" sections at /design-slice or /slice time for inherited methodology lessons. The slice-013 → slice-014 lesson-propagation is the canonical reference instance.

- **The N=6 PMI-1 versioned-gate supersession-event counter termination at slice-014 is the model for retiring mechanical-friction methodology patterns.** The pattern stabilized across N=6 events (slices 007..013) with ~1-min per-bump cost; refactor cost was ~30-45 min (slice-014 scope); break-even crossed at N=6+ (~6-7 cumulative minutes vs refactor cost). The retirement was clean — invariant preserved, secondary discipline ("did you bump at all?") migrated to 3-way META-1 + entry-pin + checklist composition without weakening any methodology guarantee. Generic methodology pattern: friction signals at N≥5 events stable warrant retirement-slice scoping; below N=5 the per-bump cost stays trivial.

- **EPGD-1 self-application at the discipline-retirement level (slice-014 (a) ↔ (b) duality)** is a new methodology pattern at N=1 standalone. The slice authoring the retirement of a pattern IS the canonical last application of the pattern. Distinct from EPGD-1's design-time-and-build-time application sub-modes (slice-013 codification); this is the discipline-evolution-meta sub-mode. Generic methodology insight: the slice that retires a discipline is the canonical reference instance of the discipline's last application — the discipline doesn't disappear at retirement; it has a final canonical instance documented in the retirement-slice's reflection. Awaiting cross-slice recurrence for N=2 stable (whichever future slice retires a different discipline).

Sub-mode (a) /critique post-fix-prose harmonization: B1 ACCEPTED-FIXED renamed function in TF-1 plan row 1 in same fix block as design.md Decision Option 1 + AC #1 + mid-slice smoke gate + verification plan (5 surfaces harmonized in one /critique fix block). - **Surface-context-aware pre-validation assert at call site (m-add-1 ACCEPTED-FIXED)** preserved slice-017 L1088-1090 diagnostic pattern. Empirically demonstrated at WRITTEN-FAILING phase: assertion failure produced "in-repo methodology-changelog.md v0.31.0 body missing 'Sub-mode (a)' marker — three-sub-mode pin broken" — surface_name interpolation makes failures self-explaining (Hendrickson).
- **/critique + /critique-review dual-review caught 12 findings on a small slice** — 9 first-Critic + 3 meta-Critic missed; all 12 ACCEPTED-FIXED at fix-prose; all 12 VALIDATED at /validate-slice. **13th consecutive 100% Critic-disposition accuracy slice**; running 108/108 cross-Critic-stack across slices 6-18 (extending project records).
- **Audit 4-7 empirical verification at /critique + /critique-review fix-prose** worked as designed — shippability row 16 enumerated (Audit 4); BC-1 audit clean (Audit 5); bidirectional v0.30.0 verified (Audit 6); helper-extraction asymmetry foreshadowing-decline documented (Audit 7). Empirical-verification-at-design-time discipline N=17 stable.

### Didn't work

- **Mini-CAD-1 row 3 ceremonial transition reflexively attempted** — Builder almost performed historical PASSING → WRITTEN-FAILING → PASSING transition pattern (N=8 stable at slice-017) without checking applicability to cleanup-slice context. Auto-mode classifier blocked correctly: modifying agents/critique.md would have violated slice's own pre-finish gate "no diff on agents/critique.md". The block was a SAVE, not friction. **Lesson**: mini-CAD-1 transition applies ONLY to slices modifying agents/critique.md; cleanup-slices with no agents/critique.md touch should mark row 4 PASSING from the start.
- **TF-1 plan initially incomplete on non-pytest-verifiable ACs** — original 4-row plan missed AC #3 (docstring) + AC #5 (no-diff) because both are verified via grep / git-diff rather than pytest. The /critique M1 ACCEPTED-FIXED ("delete TF-1 row 3") was correct on the meta-test-on-prose discipline but didn't address row-enumeration coverage. TF-1 audit `--strict-pre-finish` surfaced 3 violations at /build-slice Phase 6. Both Critics missed. **Lesson**: cleanup-slice TF-1 plans must enumerate non-pytest verification rows (`test_type = grep-verification` / `git-diff-verification` / `catalog-verification`) for every AC — TF-1 coverage discipline is independent of test_type.
- **Windows cp1252 console encoding recurred third time** (slice-007 + slice-016 + slice-018) at `tools/critique_review_audit.py` console output with U+2192 right-arrow. Workaround `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` applied inline both at /critique-review fix-prose AND /build-slice Phase 6 audit re-run. **Lesson at N=3 cumulative**: promotion threshold MET for `audit-tools-default-utf8-stdout` candidate. Approach for slice-019: `sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")` at module entry for all tools that emit non-ASCII characters.

### Pattern

- **Cleanup-slice methodology-recurrence layer is distinct from codification-slice slice-content layer** — codification slices (slice-009..017) had Critic catches dominated by self-application defects on own draft (recursive-self-application N=4..N=8 per slice). Cleanup slices (slice-018 first instance) have Critic catches dominated by **architectural-fix-secondary-cost** patterns (M2 helper extraction → m-add-1 surface_name diagnostic regression + m-add-2 helper-asymmetry foreshadowing-decline). The two layers require different Critic attention: codification slices need RSAD-1 self-application probes; cleanup slices need M*-secondary-cost probes. **Generalizable insight**: future cleanup slices should explicitly enumerate "if /critique proposes architectural change X to address Major Y, what are the secondary symmetry costs of X?" as a design-time audit.
- **Auto-mode classifier as a third Critic-stack-layer** — the auto-mode block at /build-slice Phase 5 (mini-CAD-1 ceremonial transition attempt) acted as a third-layer-Critic catch. Neither first-Critic nor meta-Critic surfaced the issue; the classifier's "modifying X would violate pre-finish gate Y" reasoning caught it. **Generalizable insight**: auto-mode classifier rules are an empirical extension of the Critic-stack at the build-time-action layer — they catch class violations that operate AT execution time (vs. design time at /critique + /critique-review). Future slices should NOT treat auto-mode blocks as friction; they're caches of past lessons baked into the harness.
- **TPHD-1 codification at slice-017 + slice-018 N=2 cumulative empirically pays off** at the classical-build-deviation layer — slice-017 shipped 3 classical DEVIATIONs (test-scoping flaw inheritance + prose-pin style mismatch + mechanical row order); slice-018 ships ZERO classical DEVIATIONs (DEVIATION-1 + DEVIATION-2 are methodology-recurrence layer, not classical). The codification-then-self-application loop (slice-017 codifies → slice-018 self-applies → measurable reduction at the next slice) is the **empirical validation pattern for methodology-discipline codification**.
- **DR-1 catch class diversification continues to grow** — slice-013/014/015 N=3 RPCD-1 sub-modes + slice-016/017 N=2 Wiegers coverage-symmetry + slice-018 N=3 new classes (diagnostic-quality regression + foreshadowing-decline gap + evidence-trace gap) = **N=6 cumulative classes stable** across slices 013-018. **Pattern**: meta-Critic effectiveness extends beyond original DR-1 charter (catching pattern-blindness) into a broader "catching secondary symmetry costs of architectural fixes" zone. Future calibration may want to formalize this as DR-1.1 or as a Dim 9 sub-clause refinement at N=8+ cumulative classes.



## Slice 019 (harden-diagnose-layering-evidence) — 2026-05-14

### Worked

- **Witness-driven scope discipline** (ADR-017 Option B vs Option A) — fixing the F-LAYER-bca9c001 false-positive class at the witnessed surface (`/diagnose` 03f-layering pass template) rather than at the broader graphify symbol-resolution layer kept the slice at 0.5 day with cheap reversibility + narrow blast radius + empirical-evidence basis. R-3 + escalation criteria capture the broader concern with explicit promotion path at N≥2 distinct slices. Risk-first ordering (Principle 2) empirically paid off.
- **TPHD-1 self-application at all 3 sub-modes** — N=2 → **N=3 stable** post-codification. Slice-019 IS canonical reference instance #3 across all 3 sub-modes. **ZERO classical build-time DEVIATIONs** at slice-019 (compared to slice-018's N=1) — empirically validates the TPHD-1 discipline at the build-deviation layer.
- **Mini-CAD-for-/diagnose as a generalizable surface family** — first introduction extends slice-007 CAD-1 + slice-010 mini-CAD-for-slice/SKILL.md to a third surface family. Pattern: methodology-skill surfaces with forward-synced installed copies get bidirectional sha256 byte-equal tests at the file-pair level (single-file convention per slice-007). Pattern is now N=3 surface families: agents/critique.md + skills/slice/SKILL.md + skills/diagnose/{SKILL.md, passes/03f-layering.md}.
- **Visual byte-equality of literal regex strings** between pass-template prose AND test-helper constants (per /critique M1 ACCEPTED-PENDING) as the v1 mitigation for test-helper-vs-runtime drift. Cheaper than v2 audit; promotes to enforced byte-equality at N≥3 violations recur (matches RSAD-1 / TPHD-1 / RPCD-1 prose-heuristic-defer precedent).
- **/critique-review DR-1 meta-Critic** continues high-ROI catching pattern-blindness in first-Critic. N=6 → **N=7 stable** with NEW class *Self-application-qualifier coherence on canonical-reference-instance naming* (M-add-2). Slice-019 also surfaces N=1 watch-list candidate *Schema-enum-vs-AC-prose mismatch* (M-add-1). DR-1 remains the strongest signal that codification slices benefit from a second adversarial pass.
- **Helper-extraction asymmetry foreshadowing-decline at N=2** (Fowler rule-of-three + slice-018 codified N≥2 threshold). `_extract_v033_body` is the 2nd version-body helper; promote to `_extract_version_body(content, version)` at N=3. Explicit deferral with promotion criteria.
- **Cross-Critic-stack 100% accuracy streak** at 14 consecutive slices (122/122 cumulative across slices 6-19). Strongest streak in the project.

### Didn't work

- **Builder's first inline ADR-017 RSAD-1 wording** — used "N=1 standalone post-codification" qualifier which is logically incoherent (slice-019 IS the codification slice; can't be post-codification of itself). Caught by meta-Critic M-add-2; rephrased to "canonical reference instance #1 at codification time" matching slice-015/017 wording precedent. **Pattern**: at-codification-slice self-application has a different semantic shape than post-codification self-application; Builder mental model conflated them. Watch-list for /critic-calibrate aggregation.
- **First-Critic over-filed Blocker severity** on B1 (must-not-defer #1 propagation gap). Ground-truth grep narrowed actionable scope — concern was Wiegers documentation-completeness, not OWASP correctness. Meta-Critic adjusted Blocker → Major. **Pattern**: documentation-completeness Critic findings tend to be Blocker-filed when ground-truth verification would narrow the actionable scope. N=2 cumulative across slices 13-19 (slice-016 B1 + slice-019 B1). At N=3 promotion threshold, propose Dim 4 sub-bullet refinement.

### Pattern

- **Codification slices that introduce a NEW methodology rule with witnessed N=1 evidence basis are well-suited to proactive ratchet codification** (per slice-017 TPHD-1 + slice-019 LAYER-EVID-1 pattern). User-invoked /slice argument promoting N=1 high-impact false-positive classes ahead of N=2 promotion threshold has now succeeded twice (slice-017 + slice-019 both shipped at 100% Critic-disposition accuracy + ZERO-classical-build-deviation regained). Generalizable insight: when the witness is concrete + load-bearing + reversibility=cheap, N=1 ratchet is empirically defensible.
- **N-surface schema-pin discipline generalizes across multiple rule families**: 3-surface (RSAD-1/EPGD-1/SCPD-1/RPCD-1/TPHD-1) → N-surface (LAYER-EVID-1 at N=3 in-repo + 3 installed + 1 ADR = N=7 total). The discipline scales naturally as more files participate (mini-CAD families add ship-hash forensic surfaces; ADR-pin convention adds decision surfaces).
- **TPHD-1 sub-mode (a) + (b) harmonization in same fix-block discipline empirically prevents classical build-time DEVIATIONs** in codification slices that introduce NEW test files / extend SKILL.md sections / add ADR + methodology-changelog entries. The discipline at N=3 self-application (slice-017 + slice-018 + slice-019) maintains a stable ZERO classical-build-deviation pattern except slice-018's N=1 (which was caught by tooling at execution time, not by /critique design time — auto-mode classifier + TF-1 audit acting as third-Critic-stack-layer).
- **Documentation-completeness vs structural-completeness distinction in Critic finding severity calibration** — N=2 cumulative observations. Documentation-completeness gaps (missing enumeration in design.md to defend against future regression) calibrate to Major after ground-truth narrows scope. Structural-completeness gaps (missing TF-1 rows for actual test coverage) calibrate to Blocker. Future Dim 4 sub-bullet refinement candidate at N≥3 promotion threshold.
