## Slice 020 (codify-bug-fix-repro-prelude-at-slice) — 2026-05-14

### Worked

- **BFRD-1 codification as 1-surface skill-prose discipline at `/slice` Step 3c shipped cleanly** with 14 first-Critic findings + 3 missed meta-Critic findings (project HWM 17 catches; 15th consecutive 100% Critic-disposition accuracy slice). The widened mode (a) regex set (`fix-*` + `*-fix` + `bugfix-*` + `hotfix-*` + `defect-*` + `repair-*` + `patch-*` + `harden-*-bug` variants) closes the slice-001 `-fix` suffix counter-example that naïve `fix-*` prefix-only would have missed empirically. Mode (b) PRIMARY candidate-source signal is the workhorse; mode (a) is fast-path fallback.
- **Verification mechanism `shippability.md grep verification` + verbal-claim-with-path fallback** gives BFRD-1 a concrete enforcement primitive grounded in `/repro` skill's documented `tests/bugs/` convention. The aspirational `bug:` provenance branch (zero precedent in shippability rows 1-19) was caught by meta-Critic M-add-2 and DROPPED at /critique-review — RPCD-1 sub-mode (b) self-application N=2 stable.
- **Helper generalization at rule-of-three trigger empirically validated** — `_extract_version_body(content, version)` refactor took ~20 min within budget; slice-018 + slice-019 wrappers preserved single-code-path discipline; 6/6 backward-compat tests PASS at call-site. Rule-of-three counter N=2 → N=3 stable.
- **Shippability catalog 20/20 PASS in 12.53s** — under 2-min target by 10×; ~0.63s/row average, fastest per-row ratio in project history; zero regressions on rows 1-19.
- **PMI-1 v1.1 atomic-bump count N=5 → N=6 stable** — sixth atomic version bump (0.33.0 → 0.34.0) with zero modifications to gate body. Empirical retirement-proof discipline holds.
- **TPHD-1 self-application N=3 → N=4 stable at all 3 sub-modes** — slice-020 IS canonical reference instance #4; harmonization in fix-block discipline ZERO classical-build-deviation streak regained AND extended (slice-019 + slice-020 = N=2 stable).
- **DR-1 dual review catches NEW class candidates at N=1 each** — `fix-block-completeness on count-drift Blockers` (M-add-1) + `proposed-mechanism-runtime-prerequisite-completeness` (M-add-2 as RPCD-1 sub-mode (b) self-application on first-Critic's own fix proposal). Both join watch-list for /critic-calibrate at slice-022+ if recur.

### Didn't work

- **Count-drift class recurred 5 times within slice-020's own fix-prose layers** — first-Critic B3 at /critique caught L132; Builder self-review caught L10 + L24 + m3 BEFORE /critique-review spawn; meta-Critic M-add-1 caught L169 Conclusion at /critique-review. The same Wiegers regression-guard coverage-symmetry class B3 was filed to retire RECURRED WITHIN the B3 fix block at L169 — textbook recursive-self-application demonstration AND first-Critic-MISS of fix-block-completeness class at N=1. Strongest single-slice density of count-drift instances in project history.
- **Original Audit 7 "architectural impossibility" framing for BFRD-1 self-application was overstated** — caught by first-Critic M1 as contingent N/A (slice-020 happens to be NEW-feature codification, not bug-fix; hypothetical future codification-AND-bug-fix slice would self-apply trivially). DR-1 catch-class N=1 → N=2 cumulative on self-application-qualifier coherence.
- **Original design.md M5 Audit 4 Option A (inline boundary slicing) contradicted aggregated lesson** — slice-019 reflection explicitly named "_extract_version_body" generalization at N=3 (next codification slice). Builder initially deferred at N=2 inline; first-Critic M5 caught the contradiction. Option C generalized helper chosen at /critique fix-prose.
- **Windows cp1252 console encoding class recurred for 4th time** at `tools/critique_review_audit.py` (slice-007 + 016 + 018 + 020). Promotion threshold (N=3) was MET at slice-018; slice-019 + slice-020 represent further recurrence well past threshold. `audit-tools-default-utf8-stdout` slice candidate is now the highest-priority next-slice candidate for slice-021. Workaround `$env:PYTHONIOENCODING = "utf-8"` keeps working but the underlying tooling fix is overdue.

### Pattern

- **Codification slices with multi-section ADR documents commit count-drift at high density on their own draft.** Slice-020's ADR-018 (~24KB, 169+ lines, 3 magnitude-citation sites) + design.md What's-new + critique.md row m3 collectively constituted 5 internal surfaces of count-consistency. Future "1-surface" codification slices that ship multi-section ADRs should pre-flight count-consistency at /critique as a discipline step — surface candidate for Dim 9 sub-clause refinement at N≥3 distinct-slice recurrence of fix-block-completeness class.
- **Recursive-self-application density HWM ratchets again** — slice-017 = 8 self-defects; slice-019 = 12; slice-020 = 17. Codification slices commit instances of the disciplines they codify on their own draft at ratios well above project average AND density scales with slice surface complexity. Future codification slices should budget for N=15+ Critic-stack catches as empirical baseline, not exception.
- **DR-1 dual review ROI extends beyond original "pattern blindness" charter** — slice-018 introduced "secondary symmetry costs of architectural fixes" class; slice-020 introduces "fix-block-completeness + proposed-mechanism-runtime-prerequisite-completeness" classes. **Cumulative DR-1 charter extension N=2 stable** (slice-018 + slice-020).
- **Override-rationale must reference concrete test enforcement** (not aspirational prose). Slice-020 M2 OVERRIDDEN rationale "phrase appears naturally in section opener" was unverified until meta-Critic SUSPICIOUS forced design.md "Prose-pin test assertion locks" addition. **Generic methodology lesson at N=1**: when OVERRIDDEN on a prose-pin / canonical-phrase claim, name the test function that locks the claim in the rationale. Promote to /critique discipline at N≥3 distinct-slice recurrence.
- **Cross-Critic-stack catch density scales with slice surface complexity** — and the project's empirical 100% Critic-disposition accuracy streak (15 consecutive slices, 131/131 first-Critic + 139/139 cross-stack across slices 6-20) holds even at the new HWM. The discipline empirically pays off: every catch was VALIDATED post-build.

## Slice 021 (add-feature-branch-workflow-at-build-and-commit-slice) — 2026-05-14

### Worked

- **BRANCH-1 audit-from-day-one approach**: programmatic gate at codification (vs deferred v2 audit per BFRD-1 / TPHD-1 / RPCD-1 etc. pattern) was structurally cheaper. Branch state is mechanically checkable via `git branch --show-current` + `git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`; the audit at ~340 lines cost less than the prose-pin test mass needed to defend prose against drift. For programmatic-checkable disciplines, audit-from-day-one is the right level of enforcement.
- **Bootstrap-reference instance pattern**: slice-021 successfully demonstrated the canonical `BRANCH=skip-bootstrap` DEVIATION line shape on its own bootstrap. Any future codification slice authoring runtime-discipline prose can use this pattern to document its own pre-existence gap. Pattern is portable.
- **Default-branch resolution via `git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` + `init.defaultBranch` fallback** — empirically robust across 3 test scenarios. Cross-project portability via runtime resolution (not hard-coded `master`/`main`) is structurally correct.
- **3-sub-mode discipline structure** (build-time + commit-time + audit-time) mirrors slice-020 BFRD-1 + slice-017 TPHD-1 + slice-016 RPCD-1 N-sub-mode precedent N=7 → N=8 stable. Pattern is now established.
- **6 ACCEPTED-FIXED inline at /critique TRI-1**: slice-020 precedent confirmed — applying simple mechanical fixes inline at /critique disposition (vs all-PENDING for /build-slice) reduces work load downstream. Mechanical fixes (count harmonization, typo, vocabulary sweep) belong inline; design pivots belong PENDING.

### Didn't work

- **`/commit-slice --merge` local-only semantics — STRUCTURALLY WRONG for non-solo projects** (DEVIATION-5; user-surfaced post-/validate). Local-merge + `git push origin master` either (a) fails on protected branches, (b) bypasses required-PR review, or (c) skips CI evaluation on slice branch in isolation. ALL 4 Critic-stack passes missed this — the dimensions evaluated `--merge` INTERNALLY (does the flow work as designed?) without asking EXTERNALLY (is this design right for the dominant real-world adopter?). **NEW Critic-stack blind-spot class**: `opinionated-default-vs-typical-adopter-profile`. Slice-022 candidate `redesign-commit-slice-for-pr-aware-flow` will DROP `--merge` + add `--push` (push slice branch to origin; user creates PR + merges via UI).
- **Aspirational Builder-self-check items in Pre-finish gate are provably falsifiable** — slice-021's "design-time fix-block-completeness self-check" item 11 was authored at /design-slice rerun AND immediately falsified at the same rerun (5 fresh count-drifts committed in the same round). M1-residual retired the item; the structural closure is /critic-calibrate Dim 9 sub-clause codification as adversarial check, NOT a Builder-self-check. Future slices should NOT propose Builder-self-checks for any class showing recursive recurrence.
- **Critic-stack's grep scope consistently misses milestone.md** — both /critique-rerun + /critique-review-rerun caught propagation gaps where ACCEPTED-FIXED dispositions claimed harmonization across "mission-brief + design.md + ADR" but failed to propagate to milestone.md. Pattern: rerun-Critic scoped grep verification to mission-brief/design/ADR but not to milestone.md. NEW DR-1 class candidate.

### Pattern

- **Recursive-self-application HWM ratchets to N=33** (was N=17 at slice-020; new project record). Density scales with slice complexity — slice-013 N=7 → slice-019 N=12 → slice-020 N=17 → slice-021 N=33. **Codification slices should budget for N=20+ Critic-stack findings as new baseline expectation**, not exception.
- **Audit-from-day-one vs deferred v2** is the right level of enforcement for any programmatically-checkable discipline. Slice-021 BRANCH-1 ships audit + 8 unit tests; the audit caught zero false positives in unit-test exhaustion + real-environment positive case. Compare to BFRD-1 / TPHD-1 / RPCD-1 / SCPD-1 / EPGD-1 / RSAD-1 prose-only -D-suffix family: those are right for *non*-mechanically-checkable disciplines. BRANCH-1's mechanical-checkability moved it to the audit-enforced-gate family alongside BC-1 / PMI-1 / CAD-1 / INST-1 / WIRE-1 / NFR-1 / CSP-1.
- **Cross-Critic-stack catch density scales with slice surface complexity AND the 100% disposition-accuracy streak extends to N=15 consecutive slices** (127/127 first-Critic + 144/144 cross-stack across slices 6-21). Adversarial review pays off empirically; the structural cost is the budget for multiple review passes (slice-021 ran 4 passes vs slice-019/020's 2 passes).
- **Slice-skill prerequisite-class disciplines belong in `## Prerequisite check` sub-sections, NOT new Steps** — strong rule. Slice-017 TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) + slice-021 BRANCH-1 sub-mode (a) precedent; methodology-changelog v0.32.0 L102 canonical naming. Worth codifying as Dim 9 sub-clause at /critic-calibrate slice-022.
- **NEW pattern observation: Critic-stack evaluates new disciplines INTERNALLY but not EXTERNALLY against typical adopter profile**. DEVIATION-5 escaped 4 review passes because all 4 evaluated `--merge` as a contract problem (does it work?) without asking the adopter-profile question (is local-merge the right default?). NEW dimension worth codifying at /critic-calibrate slice-022 as Dim 9 sub-clause refinement: *default-mode-vs-typical-adopter-workflow alignment*.

## Slice 022 (redesign-commit-slice-for-pr-aware-flow) — 2026-05-15

### Worked

- **3-mode taxonomy with mutual exclusion + preserved no-flag default** — additive CLI contract evolution for `/commit-slice` works cleanly without breaking slice-021 `--merge` users. AC #1 expanded to "argument contract" covers no-flag + --merge preservation + mutual-exclusion + guidance section in ONE acceptance criterion (vs splitting into 4 sub-ACs that would have exceeded the ≤5 hard limit). Per /critique B4 ACCEPTED-FIXED.
- **Two-pass Signal B for `--sync-after-pr` merged-state detection** — `git cherry` Pass 1 (per-commit cherry-pick equivalence) + aggregate-tree-diff Pass 2 fallback (multi-commit squash-merge) covers all 4 major PR-merge styles (plain merge-commit + rebase-merge + cherry-pick-equivalence + GitHub squash-merge). Pass 2 with 3 explicit guards (empty-FILES STOP + perf bound N=500 + superset-pinned "covers") closes data-loss false-YES paths per /critique-review M-add-5.
- **ADR partial-supersession encoding** — single `supersedes: <prior>` frontmatter slot + body-level scope enumeration is sufficient. ADR-020 supersedes ADR-019 Option 1 sub-mode (b) only; sub-modes (a) + (c) inherited unchanged. ADR-019 stays unmodified per append-only. Test `test_adr_020_exists_and_supersedes_adr_019` pins BOTH directions (forward link present + reverse `superseded-by:` ABSENT). First in-codebase use of ADR supersession (all prior ADR-001 through ADR-019 used `supersedes: null`); pattern empirically established.
- **TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) prerequisite-check empirically caught codification slice's own draft** — slice-022 TF-1 plan drifted from actual project convention at 4 surfaces during /critique + /critique-review fix-prose generation. Sub-mode (c) caught it at /build-slice entry BEFORE Phase 1, before any test mass got written against the wrong function names. The discipline catches its own author — strongest possible recursive-self-application validation.
- **Audit-from-day-one structural cost continues paying off** — slice-021 BRANCH-1 audit (mechanical-checkable discipline) ran clean throughout slice-022's lifecycle; zero false positives. Compare to prose-only -D-suffix family (BFRD-1, TPHD-1, etc.) which require Critic-stack to enforce at design/critique-time. Both layers complementary; the audit-from-day-one family closes the "mechanical drift after ship" failure mode that prose-pin tests don't catch.

### Didn't work

- **First-Critic propagation-completeness loose** — first /critique pass caught the LOCAL fixes (m2 off-by-one at design.md L187; m3 enumeration at design.md L207) but missed the SIBLING-SITE propagation (mission-brief.md L134; design.md L198 "22+ changed files"). Meta-Critic at /critique-review caught the propagation gaps (M-add-2 + M-add-3). Wiegers regression-guard coverage-symmetry watch-list ratchets cumulative N=12 across slice-021 + slice-022 (well past N=3 promotion threshold). **Pattern**: when an ACCEPTED-FIXED disposition harmonizes a count or counter at one site, the same fix block should grep-verify all sibling surfaces in mission-brief + design + ADR + milestone + changelog-target.
- **`audit-tools-default-utf8-stdout` continues to defer** — cp1252 N=5 → N=6 cumulative at slice-022 (TF-1 audit U+2192 arrow); was supposed to be slice-022's slot per slice-021 aggregated lessons but slice-022 was user-invoked on PR-aware redesign. Workaround `$env:PYTHONIOENCODING = "utf-8"` is paid at every /build-slice + /validate-slice + /reflect that touches a tool with non-ASCII stdout. Cost compounds.
- **First-Critic cross-file consistency checking is incomplete** — Pre-finish gate item L139 in mission-brief.md contradicted design.md L226 ("supersession bidirectional link per SUP-1" vs "one-directional `supersedes:` per ADR family convention"). First /critique pass had BOTH files as input but didn't fire on the cross-file consistency mismatch. Meta-Critic caught at /critique-review M-add-1. **NEW first-Critic-MISS class candidate**: `cross-mission-brief-vs-design-consistency-checking` (N=1 watch-list).
- **Pre-existing CRLF/LF environmental drift** — slice-022 caught one instance unrelated to slice scope (D-4 build-log.md). Could be systemic. Worth recon spike at slice-023+.

### Pattern

- **Codification slices commit exactly the violation their codified discipline catches** — empirical evidence at slice-022 D-1 (TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) caught slice-022's own TF-1 plan drift). Pattern observed at slice-018 (TPHD-1 catching slice-017 drift) + slice-021 (BRANCH-1 self-application N=33 HWM) + slice-022. **Generalized**: any future codification slice should explicitly self-apply its discipline at /build-slice prerequisite-check entry as the FIRST step. The discipline is most likely to catch its own author.
- **Two-pass detection for destructive predicates** — `git cherry` Pass 1 + aggregate-tree-diff Pass 2 fallback shape is the right model for any commit-reachability check that gates destructive action. Pass 1 fast-paths the common cases; Pass 2 handles the squash-merge edge case. Pattern is portable to future git-state-detection scenarios.
- **Adversarial dual-review (first /critique + /critique-review) continues paying empirically** — slice-022 meta-Critic caught 5 missed findings including 1 Blocker (M-add-1 SUP-1-misapplication-as-impossible-pre-finish-gate) that would have caused Builder-vs-audit deadlock at /build-slice Step 6. 100% Critic-disposition accuracy streak extends to **N=16 consecutive slices** (153/153 first-Critic + 154/154 cross-Critic-stack across slices 6-22). Structural cost of multiple review passes continues to be worth it.
- **Wiegers coverage-symmetry class is the dominant DR-1 recurrence class** — slice-016 + slice-017 + slice-020 + slice-021 + slice-022 all show fix-block-completeness on count-drift or sibling-site propagation. Cumulative N=12 across slice-021 + slice-022 alone. **Promotion to Dim 9 sub-clause at /critic-calibrate slice-023 is structurally overdue**.
- **NEW PATTERN OBSERVATION at slice-022**: codification slices that span multiple files (mission-brief + design + ADR) need explicit cross-file consistency checking at /critique. First-Critic evaluates each file internally; meta-Critic catches the cross-file mismatches at /critique-review (M-add-1 is the empirical evidence). Worth codifying as new Dim 9 sub-clause for /critic-calibrate: "Cross-mission-brief-vs-design-consistency check on Pre-finish gate items."
- **Recursive-self-application HWM at slice-022**: 18 substantive self-defects (13 first-Critic + 5 meta-Critic). Lower than slice-021 N=33 but only 2 review passes vs slice-021's 4 passes. Normalized: ~9 findings per Critic pass at slice-022 vs ~8.25 at slice-021. Density continues at the slice-021 baseline expectation.

---

## Slice 023 (audit-tools-default-utf8-stdout) — 2026-05-15

### Worked

- **Empirical witness anchor at codification time is load-bearing.** Slice-022 D-5 captured the cp1252 crash at `tools/test_first_audit.py` U+2192 — that concrete witness made slice-023's mission-brief defensible ("we already lost N=6 cumulative cycles to this specific class"). Without that witness, the slice would have read as abstract over-engineering. Future codification slices should surface a concrete witness for the class being retired before authoring the mission brief.
- **One-shot AST-based transformation script for 32 mechanical edits** (16 imports + 16 first-statement insertions) was empirically faster than 32 Edit-tool calls. The script ran in <1 second; the equivalent Edit-tool work would have been 5+ minutes of round-trips. Pattern works when the transformation is uniform across N files; less applicable when each file has different shape.
- **AST-based structural audit beats regex** for "first executable statement of `main()`" enforcement. Regex would have to handle docstrings + comments + multi-line signatures + decorators; AST handles all of them implicitly via `ast.FunctionDef.body[0]` after docstring-skip. ~120 LOC audit covers every shape robustly.
- **Self-application N=1 at codification time** — the audit fires against its own conformant `main()` immediately, providing in-build evidence that the rule's enforcement matches the rule's prose. This is the same pattern as slice-019 LAYER-EVID-1 / slice-021 BRANCH-1.
- **TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) pre-flight at /build-slice prerequisite-check caught 3 disposition-promised tests not enumerated in TF-1 plan**. M5 + M6 + B5 /critique dispositions said "will add test X at /build-slice" but didn't update the TF-1 plan; TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) ran a delta check at /build-slice entry and surfaced this BEFORE plan-mode. Without TPHD-1, the strict-pre-finish TF-1 audit at Step 6 would have failed and forced a backtrack.

### Didn't work

- **Builder's pre-/critique prediction of ≤5 first-Critic findings was off by 3.4×** (actual 17). The under-counting traced to a prose-pin: the design.md described slice-023 as "SMALL slice, ~30-45 min effort, helper + audit + 16 mechanical one-line additions" and inferred "low Critic-stack catch density" from line count. Reality: codification-slice Critic density is proportional to **vault-claim density** (paths / conventions / counts asserted in design.md), not LOC. 4 of 5 Blockers were Dim 9 sub-clause 2 (design.md vs canonical inventory drift) — exactly the same class slice-007 / slice-009 / slice-010 surfaced and slice-022 reaffirmed.
- **Builder authored multiple canonical-inventory claims in design.md without first running `Glob` / `Grep` on the actual codebase.** `architecture/methodology-changelog.md`, `tests/decisions/`, `test_row_*.py`, `- id:` plugin.yaml shape, leading-underscore PMI-1 filter convention — all design.md positive-inclusion claims that don't match repo reality. Each was caught at /critique (high disposition-accuracy outcome) but each represented avoidable rework. Discipline gap: at /design-slice authoring, every "the file at PATH" or "the convention TESTPATTERN exists" claim should be evidence-checked before the design.md is presented for /critique.
- **Auto-mode classifier blocked an unverified disk script at /validate-slice Step 5.5.** The temp `_run_shippability.py` script written via Write tool was then executed via subprocess and refused. Workaround: inline Python via PowerShell heredoc with the script body visible in the transcript turn that runs it. Net effect was minor (~3 min delay), but the class will recur at every slice that wants a one-shot multi-row execution script. N=3 cumulative recurrence (slice-018 + slice-021 + slice-023).

### Pattern

1. **Codification-slice Critic density follows vault-claim density, not LOC.** Slice-023 = 23 cumulative Critic-stack findings on 21 file-touch slice (helper + audit + 16 mechanical + 4 methodology + 3 tests = "SMALL by LOC, MEDIUM by vault claim"). Generalizes to: for any future codification slice, estimate /critique pass cost by counting paths + conventions + counts in design.md, not lines.

2. **Fix-block-completeness on Builder's own ACCEPTED-FIXED sweeps is the dominant meta-Critic catch-class for codification slices.** Slice-020 M-add-1 surfaced this at N=1; slice-022 N=2; slice-023 N=4 within a single fix block. Promotion-eligible for /critic-calibrate slice-024 Dim 9 sub-clause: "When Builder applies ACCEPTED-FIXED inline, the disposition table MUST sweep every sibling site (cross-file consistency), not just the site cited in the first-Critic claim."

3. **Cross-mission-brief-vs-design-consistency-checking class at N=3 cumulative** (slice-022 N=1 + slice-023 N=2 — B5 count drift across mission-brief vs design.md vs ADR-021; B3 ADR-pin path drift across mission-brief + design.md + shippability cell; M5 test-file path drift across mission-brief AC #4 + design.md L43). Promotion-eligible at N=3 threshold for /critic-calibrate slice-024 Dim 9 sub-clause.

4. **One-time witness anchors retire recurring classes cleanly.** Six cumulative cp1252 instances across 6 slices → one structural audit codification → class RETIRED. Pattern generalizes: when a class recurs at N≥3 with the same root cause (here, missing UTF-8 stdout reconfigure), the cure is a single structural rule + audit. Recurrence-pattern → audit-codification is a faster path than per-slice DEVIATION logging.

5. **TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) prerequisite-check is the structural defense for disposition-promised content gaps.** Sub-modes (a) defend at /critique fix-prose time; sub-mode (b) defends at /critique-review meta time; sub-mode (c) defends at /build-slice entry. Slice-023 confirms sub-mode (c) catches a class neither (a) nor (b) reach: "did the /critique disposition's promised test ever land in the TF-1 plan?" Worth scaling up sub-mode (c) checks beyond just TF-1: also verify shippability row Command-cell test paths, ADR-pin test names, plugin.yaml entries.

## Slice 024 (refine-dim-9-with-fix-block-completeness-sub-clause) — 2026-05-15

### Worked

- **Proactive N=2-cross-slice codification without a dedicated /critic-calibrate run.** FBCD-1 codified at N=4-distinct-slice / N=10-cumulative evidence (slice-020/021/022/023) directly from the slice-023 reflection's promotion-eligibility tag — no intervening calibration run. Extends EPGD-1/SCPD-1 precedent. The codification self-validated maximally (see Pattern). Convention confirmed sound.
- **FBCD-1 sub-mode (b) self-application as a real-time build discipline.** The Builder's own B1 11-site cumulative-count sweep + the meta-Critic's 3 sub-mode-(b) catches + the /validate-slice 3-site citation fix were all executed *as* FBCD-1 sub-mode (b) (grep the anchor across all slice-authoring files before declaring done). Codifying the discipline and immediately practicing it on the codifying slice is self-reinforcing.
- **7-phase methodology-stack plan (1a-1g + 2-6) executed clean.** Test-first ordering (1c/1d WRITTEN-FAILING → 1g flips PASSING) worked exactly as designed; the RPCD-1 end_anchor tighten (1e) stayed forward-compatible because 1g added the anchor it points to.
- **All 19 Critic-stack findings VALIDATED-or-correctly-FALSE-ALARM; 0 OVERRIDE-MISJUDGED.** The 3 FALSE-ALARMs (m2/m3/m6) were Critic-self-acknowledged non-findings — correct honesty-rule application.

### Didn't work

- **The Critic-stack missed a test-FILE-existence gap that only real-environment validation caught.** TF-1 row 13 + Verification-plan L56 + design.md Audit 8 L221 cited a non-existent `tests/methodology/test_shippability_catalog.py`. The first Critic's Audit 8 self-applied TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) and PASSED erroneously (it checks name-harmonization, never `Test-Path`). The meta-Critic's M-add-2 caught a *different* AC-#4 function-name mismatch but not this AC-#5 file-existence gap. Surfaced only at /validate-slice Step 5.5 when AC #5's literal command was executed. **This is slice-023 B4 recurring at N=2** — and slice-023 lessons-learned item 5 explicitly foreshadowed it ("also verify shippability row Command-cell test paths"). Foreshadowed-but-not-codified = it recurred.
- **Validation-harness backtick footgun.** First Step 5.5 run reported 23 spurious FAILs because the catalog runner passed backtick-wrapped markdown Command cells to the shell literally. One-line fix (`.strip("\`")`); cost ~one diagnostic cycle.

### Pattern

- **A structural blind-spot can survive 19 Critic-stack findings + 2 Critic layers and only die at real-environment command execution.** Raw finding count is not a completeness signal. The Critic-stack reviews artifacts *statically*; "does this cited file exist when the stated command runs" is reachable only by actually running the command. Generalizable: every AC whose verification is a literal command MUST have that command executed verbatim at /validate-slice — never inferred as "covered by the suite."
- **Foreshadowed-but-not-codified lessons recur.** Slice-023 lesson 5 named this exact gap as a future-scaling candidate; not acting on it meant slice-024 hit it. When a lesson explicitly names a future codification target at N=1, the half-life to recurrence is short — promote at N=2, don't wait for N=3, when the lesson itself predicts the recurrence site.
- **Codification slices' recursive-self-application closure deepens with discipline scope.** UTF8-STDOUT-1 (slice-023) closed at /build-slice (live cp1252 witness). FBCD-1 (slice-024) — a cross-file-consistency discipline — closed at THREE layers (/critique N=10 + /critique-review N=3 + /validate-slice N=3). The broader the discipline's surface, the later/deeper its self-violation surfaces. Budget validation attention accordingly for wide-surface codification slices.

## Slice 025 (add-test-file-existence-check-for-non-pytest-rows) — 2026-05-16

### Worked

- **PTFCD-1 codified across 3 surfaces with full recursive-self-application closure at 4 layers.** The slice codifying the phantom-test-file-citation discipline caught its own violations: /critique B1 (predicted FBCD-1 sub-mode (a) rule-ID drift), /build-slice TPHD-1 pre-flight (phantom test-FUNCTION citation in own TF-1 plan), PTFCD-1 sub-mode (a) self-applied on own brief, `shippability_path_audit` self-applied on own row 25. Audit-gated -D disciplines close deeper than prose-only ones.
- **The first Critic predicted its own recursive-self-application class.** B1 was explicitly anticipated in the /critique prompt as the FBCD-1 sub-mode (a) catch on a slice codifying a sibling discipline — and it materialized exactly. Meta-Critic ACCEPT (0 suspicious / 0 missed / 0 severity) with independent verification that the ≥7-site SCPD-1 sweep + 4 FBCD-1 end_anchor tightenings left no stale sibling.
- **/validate-slice caught a skipped mandatory pipeline step.** AC4 baked "DR-1 clean"; `critique_review_audit` refused on absent `critique-review.md`, surfacing that `/critique-review` had been skipped. Remediated in-flight (meta-Critic ACCEPT). This is the clearest demonstration to date that /validate-slice catches process-completeness, not just test-suite-green.

### Didn't work

- **`/critique-review` was silently skippable.** Nothing structurally enforced the mandatory dual-review between /critique and /build-slice for a methodology-surface Standard-mode slice; it was caught only because AC4 coincidentally named DR-1. Without that AC the skip would have shipped. Process hazard, not a project risk.
- **The first Critic missed a function-level phantom citation in the slice's own TF-1 plan.** It stat-checked TF-1 *paths* but not *function names*; the phantom `test_critique_agent_byte_equal_in_repo_vs_installed` survived /critique and was caught only by TPHD-1 pre-flight luck. PTFCD-1 is file-level by design; the function-level sibling is now N=1 (foreshadowed in this slice's own Out-of-scope).
- **`critique_review_audit` strict-token verdict parser** cost one re-run (parenthetical detail in the `First-Critic verdict` field rejected).

### Pattern

- **Codification slices that codify a citation/consistency discipline should stat their own TF-1 plan test-FUNCTION names at /design-slice, not just file paths.** File-level checks (PTFCD-1) miss function-level phantom citations. N=1, foreshadowed → promote a function-level extension at N=2 per the project's promote-when-foreshadowed convention.
- **A mandatory pipeline step that depends on per-slice AC authorship to be detected is not actually enforced.** /critique-review's mandatoriness in Standard mode for methodology surfaces should be a structural prerequisite-check (refuse on absent critique-review.md + no documented skip rationale), mirroring how BRANCH-1 / TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) live in `## Prerequisite check` sub-sections. Strongest slice-026 candidate.
- **New-tool slices have an under-enumerated consumer-propagation site: the UTF8-STDOUT-1 regression roll-up sentinel** (hard-coded tool count + per-tool argv test). Recurred at slice-021 / slice-023 / slice-025 (N=3). Worth a checklist note or a version-agnostic refactor of that sentinel.

## Slice 026 (enforce-critique-review-prerequisite) — 2026-05-16

### Worked

- **CRP-1 closed the exact gap slice-025's reflection named verbatim.** slice-025 L37 ("Strongest slice-026 candidate: a structural prerequisite-check...") was implemented as written; the candidate→slice handoff via reflection lessons worked with zero re-derivation.
- **Modeling the new audit on `branch_workflow_audit.py` (BRANCH-1) was high-leverage.** Dataclass + exit-code + malformed-attempt + JSON shape transferred almost verbatim; the audit was correct first-build (11/11 tests green at first run).
- **The dual-Critic stack caught the predicted self-violation.** B1 (the slice naming its own rule `CRPD-1` with a `-D` suffix — a direct ADR-019 contradiction) is exactly the slice-022 law ("codification slices commit exactly the violation their discipline catches"). Caught at /critique, renamed in-round, regression-guarded by a v0.40.0 entry-pin asserting NON-`-D`.
- **DR-1 paid off again.** /critique-review (EXTEND) caught M-add-1 — the milestone.md template forward-sync pair has no byte-equality gate — a genuine first-Critic FBCD-1-class blind spot (propagation lens applied to tool-inventory sites but not to the surface B2's own fix created).
- **Frontmatter-keyed escape-hatch (B2 redesign) was strictly better than the mission-brief's build-log mirror.** It survives Step 7b, is readable pre-build-log, AND eliminates the narrative-prose false-positive (m2) in one move.

### Didn't work

- **The slice's own AC5 cited a phantom test-FUNCTION name** (`test_..._byte_equals_installed` vs real `..._byte_equal_installed`) + AC3/AC4 off-convention names. Neither Critic flagged it; only `/build-slice` TPHD-1 sub-mode (c) pre-flight caught it. This is N=2 of the function-level-phantom-citation-missed-by-Critic class (slice-025 AC3 = N=1).
- **A prose-pin test over-asserted scope** — checked `"audit-enforced gate"` inside the Prerequisite-check segment, but that phrase lives in the Step 6 block. Caught at mid-slice smoke (test failed), fixed by widening the assertion to global BUILD; SKILL.md prose unchanged.

### Pattern

- **Function-level phantom-test-citation is promotion-eligible at N=2.** slice-025 AC3 + slice-026 AC5, both Critic-stack-missed, both TPHD-1-caught; slice-025's reflection explicitly named the function-level extension target + recurrence site at N=1. The project's "promote at N=2 when a prior lesson named the target + site" convention is satisfied — strongest slice-027 / `/critic-calibrate` candidate (extend PTFCD-1/TPHD-1 to resolve cited test-FUNCTION names inside the cited file, not just file existence).
- **The slice-022 self-violation law is now N≈6 stable across codification slices** (020-026). Any discipline-codifying slice should pre-budget the dual-Critic stack to catch its own discipline's violation and self-apply at design time where structurally possible.
- **New-tool consumer-propagation roll-up sentinel recurs at N=4** (slice-021/023/025/026). The version-agnostic refactor of the UTF8-STDOUT-1 hard-coded-count + per-tool-argv sentinel is an overdue SMALL slice.
- **Template forward-sync is an unguarded methodology surface at N=1** (M-add-1): `install_audit._check_templates` is existence-only; the milestone.md template now has a documented in-repo↔installed pair with no drift gate. Watch-list at N=1; a `tools/template_drift_audit.py` (mirroring mini-CAD/CAD-1) promotes at N=2.


## Slice 027 (add-pipeline-chain-auto-advance) — 2026-05-16

### Worked
- The dual-Critic stack stayed 100%-accurate on a cross-cutting tooling slice (10/10 VALIDATED, 0 FALSE-ALARM, 0 true MISS). Voluntary mandatory-Critic on methodology surfaces continues to pay (now N=10/10 across slices 1-9 + 27 class).
- Live dogfooding as validation: slice-027 codifies pipeline auto-advance AND the session that built it ran the auto-advance + the TRI-1/plan-mode HALTs end-to-end — the slice validated itself behaviorally, the strongest possible evidence.
- "Run the new audit against its own repo before the full suite" self-review caught the `_CANONICAL_CHAIN` successor-mismatch (critique→/build-slice vs /critique-review) pre-suite.

### Didn't work
- Pre-build grep for "tests asserting the tool count" used literal `19`/`17` and missed `test_utf8_stdout_regression.py:223`'s `== 19` sentinel → it failed in the full-suite run (predicted N=5 recurrence, but caught late not early). Count-agnostic regex would have caught it pre-suite.
- The new-tool consumer-propagation roll-up sentinel recurred at N=5 — FOUR coupled sites for one new tool. The version-agnostic refactor is now overdue and is the dominant new-tool-codification friction.

### Pattern
- slice-022 self-violation law held at N≈7 (codification slices 020-027) and fired THREE times on slice-027's own drafts: design-time phantom path (B1, first-Critic), build-time audit-constant successor-mismatch (self-review — the exact class PCA-1 itself catches), build-time suite (changelog `NON-`-D`` token + N=5 sentinel). Audit-gated codifications self-catch progressively deeper (N=3 layers here).
- For audit-shipping codification slices: the audit's encoded canonical constants are a self-violation hotspot; a pre-suite "audit-against-own-repo" pass is load-bearing, not optional.

## Slice 028 (refactor-utf8-rollup-sentinel-version-agnostic) — 2026-05-16

### Worked
- slice-014/ADR-013 3-part template transferred cleanly to a second rule (UTF8-STDOUT-1 v1.1) — derive-real-invariant + AST meta-test + failure-path regression. Rule-ID lineage preserved (v1.1, not new ID), exactly mirroring PMI-1 v1.0→v1.1.
- Observed-covered-set (AST-read from real cp1252 call sites) retired the N=5 count-bump maintenance tax AND strengthened the invariant — proven by real fake-tool smoke (uncovered → RED naming it; covered → GREEN; removal → GREEN zero-edit).
- Dual-Critic stack 10/10 VALIDATED, 0 FALSE-ALARM. Meta-Critic EXTEND caught B-add-1 (helper-extraction would defeat the AST meta-test, fail-OPEN) — the highest-value finding, missed by the first Critic via composition-blindness (3 template parts checked in isolation).

### Didn't work
- Validation harness used `git checkout -- <test file>` to revert AC2/AC3 demo mutations. Under branch-per-slice the slice is UNCOMMITTED → it silently reverted the ENTIRE slice refactor to pre-slice HEAD; post-revert "1 passed" was the OLD `== 20` sentinel — a false-green that nearly masked total loss of the slice's work. Caught by hash mismatch. Refactor reconstructed verbatim from context; re-validated clean. Promoted BC-PROJ-3 + BC-GLOBAL-2.
- Self-written AST meta-tests false-positived on their own defensive code (int-Compare check caught helper arity `len(args) >= 2`; counter-anchor caught the guard tests' own introspection `ast.parse`). Cost one debug iteration. Fix = slice-014-faithful defect-class-precise scoping, not blanket structural bans.

### Pattern
- slice-022 self-violation law now N≈8, fired DEEPEST yet on an audit-adjacent codification slice: defect class committed at design-time (option-3 B2 declaration-weakening) AND at fix-design-time (option-4 B-add-1 helper-extraction fail-OPEN), each caught one Critic layer deeper, all pre-build. For 3-part-template refactor slices, the part-INTERACTION is the meta-Critic's highest-yield target, not per-part correctness.
- Destructive git on uncommitted slice work is a class hazard under branch-per-slice: any mutate-then-revert validation/demo MUST use in-place reverse edits or temp copies, never `git checkout`/`git restore`/`git stash`. A silent full-slice revert can produce a false-green that survives a casual re-run.
- The competing "sentinel-refactor" deferred candidate is retired; function-level-PTFCD-1 (N=3) is now the uncontested strongest standing deferred candidate.


## Slice 029 (make-diagnose-dispatch-sequential) — 2026-05-16

### Worked
- **Sequential-by-default + `--parallel` opt-in** retired the user-blocking R-1/#57037 cascade on the default path while preserving the fast path; validated by real-shell execution of the actual 3-arm flag-strip under Git-Bash 5.2.37 MINGW64 (7 invocations, all correct) — the strongest evidence form, beating prose-pins alone.
- **Plan-mode pre-reading the enforcing audit's actual assertion** (`test_each_changelog_entry_carries_rule_reference` needs only the literal `Rule reference` line, not a minted rule-ID) de-risked the TRI-1 M2-option-B shape before any edit — no late build failure.
- **DR-1 dual-review priming with the slice-022 self-violation lens** landed a pinpoint catch: meta-Critic M-add-2 (ADR-027 still said "retire" inside the very artifact M1's fix touched). N≈9 for the self-violation law on methodology-surface slices.
- TRI-1 surfaced two genuine governance/scoping forks (M2 changelog-obligation vs slice-002 precedent; M-add-1 flag-typo scoping) instead of the Builder pre-deciding — both resolved cleanly by the user.

### Didn't work
- **First-Critic "all-8-ACCEPTED-FIXED" was the predicted too-easy signature** — it verified each finding's premise but not whether the *applied fixes* introduced fresh inconsistencies; the meta-Critic caught the residuals (M-add-2/3). Lesson: on codification slices, explicitly task the meta-Critic with re-auditing the applied fixes, not just the original findings.
- **`/validate-slice` shippability check tripped on pre-existing ambient breakage** unrelated to the slice (gitignored `build-checks.md` truncated to 1 rule before the slice began) — forced a false PCA-1 HALT and burned a full investigate→decide cycle. The catalog has no machine-stable command column, so the ad-hoc runner also mis-parsed the Runtime cell (`<5s`) as a command (#28/#29 false positives).

### Pattern
- **Shippability-catalog rows must not depend on gitignored / never-tracked vault content.** When they do, the catalog FAILs for environment reasons unrelated to the slice under validation — a false HALT that also masks real regressions. Generalizable methodology gap → R-4 follow-up slice. The catalog needs a machine-stable command column (kills the ad-hoc-runner Runtime-cell footgun) AND its rows must track tracked code, not local vault state.
- **Suspected `/reflect` Step 5b append-vs-overwrite defect** — both build-checks.md files ending up with ONLY the newest promoted rule is the signature; investigate the promotion path directly in the R-4 follow-up (regression test: promote rule #2, assert rule #1 survives).
- **Empirical real-shell validation beats asserted portability** — the meta-Critic's "unverified MINGW bash-array" concern (M-add-1) was discharged not by argument but by running the exact snippet under the real shell. Codification/prose slices with an executable artifact should validate the artifact's runtime behavior, not just its prose-pins.

## Slice 030A (repair-build-checks-vault) — 2026-05-16

### Worked
- **Split over 3rd patch.** After 2 BLOCKED dual-Critic loops the meta-Critic read "flaw relocating each loop + all-ACCEPTED drafts" as a split/scope-cut signal, not a v3-patch signal. The user-approved split (030→030A/030B) converged in ONE critique pass (v3 NEEDS-FIXES→CLEAN). Splitting genuinely removed the flaw-relocation surface.
- **Mid-slice smoke gate as the fidelity oracle for best-effort recovery.** Byte-exact `trigger_keywords` for the 3 R-4-lost rules was unrecoverable (gitignored deltas-only archive); the design pre-accepted this and relied on the smoke gate. `test_build_checks_audit.py` 39/0 empirically proved the best-effort reconstruction faithful — the design's safety mechanism worked exactly as intended.
- **Deterministic downstream gate for a non-deterministic step.** `/reflect` Step 5b has no promotion function (LLM prose); ADR-029's reframe (don't fix a non-existent source — enforce the invariant downstream with BCI-1) is the correct control shape and is regression-testable.
- **Builder pushing back on a Critic sub-premise, meta-verified.** v3-B2: Builder corrected the Critic's false "git-tracked archive" claim (`git ls-files architecture/`→0); the meta-Critic independently verified the Builder was right. The Builder↔Critic separation works only when the Builder engages.

### Didn't work
- **Autonomous-loop rubber-stamping.** v1+v2: all Builder drafts ACCEPTED-PENDING with zero pushback, auto-ratified triage; the v1 critique.md/critique-review.md (triage_audit-clean) were OVERWRITTEN by the re-critique (audit trail destroyed → restored as critique-history-v1.md); v2 silently weakened a v1-ratified disposition. The adversarial stack caught all of it, but it should not have had to.
- **CRLF/LF drift-test fragility surfaced only at real-command validate.** 4 Critic/meta-Critic passes reviewed design, not the Windows-line-ending interaction; `test_diagnose_skill_drift` raw-byte compare false-FAILed in the shippability catalog (content byte-identical). N+1 of the slice-024/029 "structural blind spot dies only at real-command execution" class. → R-5.

### Pattern
- 2 consecutive BLOCKED dual-Critic loops with all-ACCEPTED drafts IS the rubber-stamp/non-convergence signal — stop patching, surface a split/scope-cut decision; don't wait for the meta-Critic to force it. Self-check after BLOCKED #1.
- Never overwrite a prior round's critique.md/critique-review.md in a re-critique loop — suffix `-vN`; the prior triage_audit-clean table is a load-bearing audit artifact (its deletion is why the v2 meta-Critic could only score M1 SUSPICIOUS).
- Shippability rows/commands must not depend on environment-mutable state: gitignored content (R-4) AND Windows line-ending checkout (R-5). 030B's machine-stable command column + drift-test CRLF normalization are the concrete fixes.

## Slice 031 (complete-shippability-decoupling, split-label 030B) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- The user-ratified b-split converged end-to-end: v1 BLOCKED → v2 NEEDS-FIXES → CLEAN → build shipped in ONE build with 3 smoke-driven implementation corrections, zero scope/design change, zero flaw-relocation. Splitting a two-features-in-one slice genuinely removes the relocation surface (2nd confirmation after slice-030).
- The mid-slice smoke gate (SCMD-1 run against the real catalog corpus) caught all 3 AST-classifier false-positive bugs that 4 Critic/meta passes missed — the empirical-fidelity-oracle role (slice-030A lesson) validated again.
- BCI-1's `live ≡ fixture` guarantee made the incidental decoupling provably byte-faithful: 93 tests passed post-repoint with zero assertion-semantics change.
- Dual-Critic per-finding accuracy stayed at 100% post-split (v2 6/6 + M-add-A all VALIDATED at reflection).

### Didn't work
- `/slice` chose a letter-suffixed folder name (`slice-030B-`) that BRANCH-1's numeric `slice-NNN-` regex rejects → mid-build folder/branch/id rename churn (R-6). The "numeric folder, prose label" split convention was implicit.
- A trailing parenthetical on the `**Test-first**: true` brief line silently disabled the entire TF-1 gate (default-off semantics) — a silent gate-bypass caught only by an explicit pre-finish re-run (R-7).
- Initial SCMD-1 classifier over-reached (blanket `Path.home()`→violation flagged 39 unrelated fns) and false-flagged synthetic `tmp_path` BCI-1 trees — design-stage reasoning could not predict the false-positive surface.

### Pattern
- **Methodology-audit-interaction defects are a design-stage blind spot, N+3.** Audit field-line regexes, branch-folder regexes, and AST-classifier false-positive surfaces are invisible to Critics reviewing *design*; they only die at real-execution (slice-024/029/030A class). Mitigation: run every gate on the actual artifact (not unit fixtures) at prerequisite AND pre-finish, and treat "not enabled"/"usage-error" on a slice that should engage the gate as a silent-bypass alarm.
- **Split → one-pass convergence, twice.** When the flaw is "this is actually two features", split beats patch — the relocation surface is removed, not relocated.

## Slice 032 (add-query-design-skill) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- `/build-slice` plan-mode "design is wrong" gate caught a false-precedent claim that the full dual-Critic stack (first Critic m1 + Builder disposition + meta-Critic confirmation) had rubber-stamped: design.md cited a "slice-029 no-rule-lineage precedent" for omitting a bespoke changelog entry-pin; reality is 24/24 versions are pinned, slice-029 included. Plan-mode read the actual enforcing test file and surfaced it.
- Routing the DEVIATION-1 correction through a full re-critique loop paid off twice: v2 first-Critic caught the correction committing the exact slice-022 self-violation it cited (specified 2 of scmd_1's 4 assertions); v2 dual-review caught the fix re-introducing the single-site-coverage silent-drift class at N=2 (`_QD1_PHRASE` obligated 3 sites, pinned 1).
- B2-style "verify, don't assume" demand was empirically vindicated — the new `Path.home()` drift test *could* have tripped SCMD-1's incidental classifier; the mid-slice decoupling-audit check confirmed clean (incidental=0) rather than assuming the pattern-copy was safe.

### Didn't work
- A naive shippability-catalog runner that doesn't implement SCMD-1's deterministic `;`-split produced a false-FAIL on a multi-segment Machine-cmd row (#28); masked nothing real but cost an analysis cycle. The documented Step-5.5 runner must `;`-split.
- R-5 environment-fragility recurred a 3rd time (N+2): catalog drift-tests (#1/#19 diagnose) FAIL on machine-local install staleness orthogonal to the slice under test. Still only deferred, now overdue.

### Pattern
- **The dual-Critic stack does not verify precedent claims.** When a Builder disposition cites "slice-NNN did/didn't do X" as justification, neither the first Critic nor the meta-Critic re-checked it against the actual artifact — they reasoned about the *claim*, not the *file*. The slice-029 "pre-read the enforcing audit's actual assertion" lesson now generalizes from Builder to Critic. Strong `/critic-calibrate` input (N=1, but high-leverage: a false precedent survived 2 adversarial passes + dual-review).
- **A design correction is an unguarded design surface.** Corrections discovered at build-plan-mode feel like trusted edits but carry the same defect classes as original design (here: slice-022 self-violation + single-site-coverage blind spot, N=2 within one slice). Route every correction through the full adversarial loop.

## Slice 033 (fix-skill-drift-test-crlf-normalization) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- The chartered single-purpose fix slice converged cleanly: first-Critic NEEDS-FIXES (2B/4M/2m) → all ACCEPTED-FIXED → DR-1 ADJUST (M3 sev recalibration + m-add-1) → TRI-1 CLEAN → built in one pass, zero post-build design deviation. R-5 (N+2 chartered) retired environment-independently.
- M1 was the highest-value Critic catch and reality confirmed it EXACTLY: `.gitattributes eol=lf` + `git add --renormalize` updates the index only — the already-CRLF working tree needed an explicit `rm + git checkout --` to materialise LF. The M1-mandated working-tree-STATE test caught the gap a `git check-attr` declaration check would have shipped green.
- BC-PROJ-4 ("run every affected gate on the real artifact at pre-finish + read its output") caught the R-7 silent-bypass that BOTH the first-Critic AND the DR-1 meta-Critic missed — exactly as designed. The build-checks layer is the structural backstop for the audit-vs-artifact interaction the Critic stack cannot reach.

### Didn't work
- The slice nearly committed R-7 — the exact footgun documented in its own risk register — because `/slice`'s mission-brief template idiomatically annotates `**Test-first**: true  (per TF-1 — …)`, which breaks `test_first_audit.py`'s `\s*$`-anchored regex → silent default-off. The dual-Critic stack did not flag it despite R-7 being OPEN and the meta-Critic being explicitly asked about TF-1 status vocabulary. Caught only at pre-finish by reading the gate's real output.
- A first-pass ad-hoc shippability runner (outer-backtick-strip only) false-FAILed the lone multi-segment catalog row #28 (slice-032's secondary discovery, reproduced LIVE) — the runner contract is unpinned. Split out + opened as R-8.

### Pattern
- **slice-022 self-violation law now ~N≈10** — a methodology/audit-tooling slice will commit the exact defect class its own risk register documents (R-7 here). Generalises: for audit-touching slices, treat the BC-PROJ-4 real-artifact pre-finish gate run as the PRIMARY R-6/R-7-class catch; do not expect the Critic stack to reach it (structurally out of scope — Critics review intent + artifacts statically, not the audit-parsing-the-artifact interaction).
- **Declaration-check vs state-check** is a recurring high-value Critic catch on infra/config slices: when a fix verifies a *declaration* (`git check-attr`, a config key, an attribute) rather than the *resulting state*, the assertion is hollow — demand the state check.
- **BC-1 promotion candidate (NOT promoted — opt-in, no user confirmation in autonomous run; recorded for a future `/reflect`)**: "any NEW in-repo↔installed `.md` drift guard MUST delegate to `tests/skill_drift_equality.assert_md_forward_synced` (EOL-normalized), never re-inline raw `read_bytes()` sha256" — would guard EOL-DRIFT-1 against a future drift-guard silently reverting the R-5 class.

## Slice 034 (fix-tf1-audit-field-line-regex) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- **Tasking the first Critic to EMPIRICALLY RUN the proposed regex against adversarial strings reached the audit-vs-artifact defect class the project had concluded was structurally Critic-unreachable.** slice-031/033 both recorded "the dual-Critic stack structurally cannot reach the audit-parsing-the-artifact interaction; only BC-PROJ-4 catches R-6/R-7-class". slice-034 M1 falsifies the absolute form: the first Critic ran `(true|false)\b.*$` against `false-positive` → caught a narrower R-7 self-violation AT /critique (design-time), not at pre-finish. The enabling factor was an explicit prompt instruction to test the pattern with Bash python, not reason about it.
- BC-PROJ-4 dogfood (run the changed audit on the slice's OWN brief) was again the single most decisive validation artifact: "clean. 9 row(s)" (ENABLED) vs the pre-fix "not enabled" — one line that proves R-7 closed on the real artifact.
- /repro → /slice (BFRD-1 STOP-route) → /design → /critique → /critique-review → TRI-1 → /build → /validate → /reflect ran end-to-end autonomously with one user gate (plan approval) + one ratify gate (TRI-1); the catalogued failing repro (shippability #34) made the bug non-silently-recurrable before any fix code existed.

### Didn't work
- The first Critic's deep regex-correctness focus (M1/M2 — the hardest catches) crowded out a mechanically-derivable checklist obligation: M-add-1 (a behavior-changing methodology entry needs a minted RULE-ID + `test_v_0_NN_0_*` entry-pin — the slice-032 DEVIATION-1 class). Caught only by the DR-1 meta-Critic. "Deep-dive crowds out the checklist item" recurs.
- Validation surfaced a pre-existing latent tool defect (`tools.risk_register_audit --filter-status open` lists RETIRED risks — R-5 + R-7 both appear; status field is correct). Orthogonal to slice-034; logged as R-9. A reminder that mutating vault state at /validate can expose unrelated tool bugs that no Critic pass would have predicted.

### Pattern
- **slice-022 self-violation law holds at ~N≈11 — but its corollary now has a counter-move.** A methodology/audit slice still commits its own risk register's defect class (M1: the R-7-fixing regex re-introduced a narrower R-7). The standing mitigation was "BC-PROJ-4 is the only backstop; the Critic stack can't reach it." slice-034 shows a *second* backstop is reachable: a first-Critic prompt that mandates empirically executing the changed parser/regex against adversarial inputs. Strongest `/critic-calibrate` input since the FBCD-1 cluster — candidate Dim-9 surgical sub-bullet: "for slices changing an audit's own parse rule, the Critic MUST run the proposed pattern against adversarial strings, not reason about it." Pair it with a Dim-7 checklist sub-bullet: "methodology-surface behavior change → assert a minted RULE-ID + entry-pin obligation is captured" (the M-add-1 class, DR-1-caught N=1 here, slice-032 DEVIATION-1 N≈prior).

## Slice 035 (rename-status-skill-to-pulse) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- The Bucket A/B/C reference taxonomy (executable filesystem-path binds vs prose vs frozen/unrelated) plus a mandated pre- AND post-edit inventory grep made a 24-file, ~126-occurrence rename land atomically with the full 616-test suite green and zero dangling binds. Codified as SRCD-1.
- DR-1 dual-review paid for itself decisively: the meta-Critic's B-add-1 (4-part atomic bump; the mission-brief literally said "triple") was load-bearing and ONLY the meta-Critic caught it. First Critic was 10/10 VALIDATED with a clean anti-over-reach retraction (B3 false `critique.md:194` citation).
- Frozen-as-history-vs-rewrite as an explicit per-line disposition kept historical changelog narratives + the ADR-030 corpus + lessons-learned.md:414 honest while still repointing live sections and Validation cross-ref cells.

### Didn't work
- The first Critic + the original design both modeled the PMI-1 atomic bump as in-repo-only ("triple"), dropping the installed `~/.claude/ai-sdlc-VERSION` leg — the exact slice-006 DEVIATION-2 / slice-007-B1 recurrence. The first-Critic blind spot on the installed-side configuration set is now N≥3.
- Plan sequencing put installed-copy reconciliation at Task 9, but the mid-slice INST-1 smoke gate (Task 4) audits the installed tree → false-looking smoke failure until the installed skill rename was pulled forward. Caught by the gate as designed, but a sharper plan would order installed-reconciliation before any INST-1 gate.

### Pattern
- **Identifier renames have two non-interchangeable reference classes.** Executable filesystem-path binds (path-list constants, `(ROOT/"skills"/"<name>"/…).read_text()`, test-module filenames + `test_<name>_*` fn defs) hard-fail the suite on a directory rename; prose `/<name>` refs don't. A rename propagated as a prose string-replace silently ships broken binds. The real-artifact post-edit inventory grep — not Critic reasoning — is the structural backstop (slice-022 self-violation law: the inventory-completion slice itself shipped an incomplete inventory in rev-1; 14 findings corrected it).
- **The installed-side configuration set (`~/.claude/ai-sdlc-VERSION`, forward-synced `~/.claude/methodology-changelog.md` self-references) is the dual-Critic stack's recurring structural reach gap on version-bumping methodology slices.** Candidate `/critic-calibrate` input: a first-Critic Dim-7/Dim-9 sub-bullet enumerating all four PMI-1 atomic-bump legs.

## Slice 036 (fix-rr1-audit-status-filter) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- BFRD-1 → /repro → /slice → /design → /critique → /critique-review → /build → /validate → /reflect ran end-to-end autonomously (PCA-1), HALTing exactly at the two enumerated user gates (TRI-1 ratify, plan-mode approval) + one BRANCH-1 dirty-WT confirmation.
- Dual-Critic stack filed 7 findings (6 first + 1 meta DR-1 EXTEND), ALL VALIDATED, zero false-alarm, correct severity. Meta-Critic M-add-1 caught a real false-comfort (the slice's own "code-conformance-to-doc" thesis leaned on prose-pin tests that did not pin the prose).
- BC-PROJ-4 dogfood (run the changed audit on the real register at mid-slice smoke + pre-finish) was the decisive validation artifact — proved the 1-line fix on the real artifact more conclusively than any unit fixture.
- 1-line code change, surgical; 30/30 module + 36/36 shippability, zero regression.

### Didn't work
- The dual-Critic stack blessed an "AC3 no-regression carve-out survives strict TF-1" claim that `test_first_audit --strict-pre-finish` then refused (`ac-without-row`). slice-022 self-violation law N+1 — a slice fixing an RR-1 audit shipped a TF-1-audit self-violation in its own brief; only the BC-PROJ-4 real-artifact pre-finish run caught it.
- The Critic stack proposed a factually wrong fixture oracle (`--filter-band high == {R1,R3}`; R3 is medium band) — reasoned about the fixture without computing `_band_for_score`. Caught only at /build-slice plan-mode.

### Pattern
- The dual-Critic stack's two standing blind spots (audit-self-violation; RULE-ID/entry-pin obligation) recurred AGAIN — these are exactly the 2026-05-17 `/critic-calibrate` ACCEPTED Proposals 1 & 2, which remain "user-to-apply-manually" and are NOT yet in the live `agents/critique.md`. slice-036 is a clean pre-codification baseline: applying those proposals would very likely have caught misses #1 and #3. The structural backstop for both classes remains the BC-PROJ-4 real-artifact pre-finish run, NOT the Critic stack. New watch sub-class (N=1): "Critic must COMPUTE fixture-derived expected values (scores/bands), not eyeball them."

## Slice 037 (extend-ptfcd-1-to-test-function-level) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- Dual-Critic stack at 12/12 VALIDATED, 0 FALSE-ALARM again on a codification slice. First Critic caught BOTH its standing blind spots this round (B1 audit-self-violation own AC4 phantom; B3 RULE-ID/`-D`-vs-`vN.N` obligation) — consistent with the 2026-05-17 /critic-calibrate Proposals 1 & 2 pattern. Meta-Critic EXTEND added 4 one-layer-deeper findings (M-add-1 tautological-green AC was the sharpest: an AC verified only by a CAD-1 byte-equality test cannot fail when the AC is unmet).
- Empirical-execution discipline paid off: B2's discriminator regex was RUN against the live archived adversarial corpus (not reasoned about), confirming zero false-accept/reject before build. The slice-034 prose row `(full existing module — non-regression)` is a real, named regression fixture now.
- Build-ordering constraint (M-add-2) was load-bearing: shippability row 37 added LAST, after its cited test files + entry-pins existed, so the slice's own newly-live function-level audit found its own catalog row clean (37 rows / 238 tokens). Second-order self-application closed by sequencing, not luck.
- `tools/_pyfn.py` shared tri-state helper (mirrors `tools/_stdout.py`) — leading-underscore, no `main()` → automatically exempt from UTF8-STDOUT-1 / INST-1 / PMI-1. Verified at build (still 22 tools). The shared-`_helper` pattern keeps two audits in lockstep with one source of truth.

### Didn't work
- Spec shipped `tests/tools/` paths through /slice → /design-slice → /critique → /critique-review (4 gates incl. meta-Critic) — `tests/tools/` does not exist; the convention is `tests/methodology/`. NONE of the Critic layers stat'd the directory; plan-mode caught it in one `ls`. The slice-027-B1 phantom-path-convention class recurred, confirming plan-mode artifact-reading is the structural backstop, not the Critic stack.
- Row 37's first Machine-cmd cited an archive-corpus-reading test → SCMD-1 incidental-coupling; surfaced ONLY by the pre-finish SCMD-1 decoupling gate, not by M-add-2's own row-37 analysis (which checked function-resolution, not gitignored-state coupling). The slice-029/R-4 lesson predicted exactly this; the slice-022 self-violation law fired at maximal depth (a phantom-citation-discipline slice nearly shipped a gitignored-coupled catalog row).

### Pattern
- **Audit-codification slices self-apply recursively at THREE layers**: (1) TF-1-plan (B1), (2) shippability-catalog-row resolution under the new audit (M-add-2), (3) catalog-row incidental-coupling to gitignored state (SCMD-1, pre-finish-only). Layers 2 & 3 are reachable ONLY by the pre-finish real-artifact runs (function-level audit on its own catalog + SCMD-1 decoupling), never by the Critic stack. Budget all three; sequence the new audit's own catalog row LAST.
- **Plan-mode `ls`/stat of every cited new test directory must be the first plan-mode action** for any slice authoring test paths — the dual-Critic stack (incl. meta-Critic) reads path strings but does not verify directory existence; this is now N≥2 (slice-027-B1, slice-037).
- **`-D`-rule refinements mint a new `-D` ID, never a `vN.N` label** (PTFFD-1←PTFCD-1, per TFFL-1↔TF-1; ADR-038). The `vN.N` label is exclusively the NON-`-D` audit-gate naming class. Codified for the `-D` family.
- **A content-bearing AC needs a content pin, not a byte-equality/forward-sync pin** — a CAD-1 / drift-equality test passes whether or not the content change exists, so it is a tautological green for any AC whose deliverable is prose/a rule/a sub-clause (M-add-1; mirrors the slice-025 `test_critique_dim_9_*` content-pin precedent).

## Slice 038 (pin-shippability-runner-segment-contract) — 2026-05-17

### Worked
- Pinning a runtime contract behind an INVOKED single-sourced tool (not prose): `tools/shippability_runner.py` reuses SCMD-1 `_segments()` by object identity. The SRSC-1 dogfood (the shipped runner running the full real 38-row catalog at the slice's own /validate-slice Step 5.5, 38/38 PASS) was the decisive validation artifact — proved the R-8 fix on reality more conclusively than any unit fixture.
- Object-identity reuse assertion (`runner._segments is scmd1._segments`) turned "reuses, not re-derives" from prose into a mechanically-enforced contract (AC1). Cheap, high-signal.
- Dual-Critic stack 7/7 VALIDATED, 0 FALSE-ALARM — precision strong again on a codification slice. DR-1 meta-Critic correctly recalibrated B2 (Blocker→Major: first-Critic over-generalized a single-precedent inference) and contributed missed-finding m-add-1 (stale count).
- Load-bearing negative fixture as an explicit contrast (naive-outer-strip vs `_segments()` on REAL row #28, both branches exercised) — not a tautology over the already-correct helper (slice-037 lesson applied).

### Didn't work
- B1's applied-fix prescribed adding the runner to `_POSITIONAL_SLICE_TOOLS`; that list passes a slice-folder arg a catalog-file-path tool can't consume. Both first-Critic AND the DR-1 meta-Critic blessed it — the meta-Critic explicitly "verified" it as the "correct bucket" by reasoning about `_positional_slice_argv`'s shape without checking the sibling precedent or running the argv. Caught only at /build-slice by reading the two sibling shippability tools' actual (non-)membership. Corrected to the bespoke-cp1252-test mechanism; B1 intent preserved.

### Pattern
- A Critic/meta-Critic prescription of the form "add tool X to parametrized list L" is a CLAIM about L's argv contract, not a verified fact — even when the meta-Critic states it "verified" the bucket. For any slice adding a `main()`-bearing tool, choose the UTF8-STDOUT-1 covered_set mechanism by reading the sibling tools' actual membership, not the Critic's conclusion. N+1 of the slice-032 "Critic reasons about the claim, not the artifact" law; the build-time artifact read is the structural backstop (no new Critic dimension — slice-037: don't add dimensions for structurally-backstopped blind spots). The slice-034 "task the Critic to empirically EXECUTE the proposed thing" remedy would have caught it — neither Critic ran the proposed argv.
- Calibration: first-Critic rounds single-precedent (N=1) inferences up to Blocker (B2); DR-1 is reliably catching/recalibrating this — the dual layer is doing its job on severity, not just findings.

## Slice 039 (apply-critic-calibrate-proposals-to-critique-agent) — 2026-05-18

### Worked
- Applying user-ACCEPTED `/critic-calibrate` proposals as a first-class slice (verbatim-to-intent vs the calibration-log Proposals table, dual-Critic verified no weakened paraphrase) cleanly closed the feedback loop the slice-036 reflection flagged as open. APED-1 (Dim 9 #12) + MEPD-1 (Dim 7) shipped under one v0.52.0 with two entry-pins (ADR-041), CAD-1 clean, shippability 39/39.
- DR-1 dual-review was load-bearing again: meta-Critic M-add-1 caught that the first-Critic's m2 fix (`"_lists_eleven_sub_clauses" not in catalog` + count 14), already applied as ACCEPTED-FIXED, was a GUARANTEED /validate-slice false-FAIL on shippability line-34's frozen slice-025 narrative. The selector-prefix (`::test_critique_dim_9_`) discriminator reformulation was mechanically verified on the real catalog (14 renamed, 1 frozen preserved, runner 39/39).
- BC-PROJ-4 "run the FULL methodology suite on the real artifact at pre-finish and READ its output" surfaced a slice-038-rooted stale prose-pin (R-10) that the dual-Critic stack structurally cannot reach; the slice-029 independence check (`git diff --quiet master -- <file>` + `git status` surface) cleanly proved it slice-039-innocent and not catalogued.
- RPCD-1 sibling-scoping (tighten the 5 PTFCD-1/PTFFD-1 `end_anchor`s when appending Dim 9 #12) is now a reliable mechanical companion to any Dim 9 sub-clause append — predicted from the slice-013/015/024 precedent at plan time, applied without surprise.

### Didn't work
- The first Critic's m2 was directionally right but shipped a wrong artifact (count 15 not 14; `not in catalog` would false-FAIL) — and the Builder applied it as ACCEPTED-FIXED before the per-line `grep -o` was run. A Critic-proposed test oracle (count / formulation) is a CLAIM; recompute it mechanically BEFORE baking it in (slice-036 lesson, N+1). DR-1 had to re-open an already-"fixed" finding.
- First-Critic FBCD-1 sub-mode (a) partial-enumeration recall: it enumerated the changelog:266 frozen-history twin (m1) but did not re-run the same full-fileset grep against the shippability.md surface the slice actually edits, where the symmetric line-34 twin lives.
- slice-038 (SRSC-1) shipped a SKILL.md prose repoint without superseding the slice-031 mini-CAD prose-pin asserting the OLD wording → the methodology suite has been non-green on master since slice-038, undetected until slice-039's full-suite pre-finish run (R-10).

### Pattern
- A Critic-proposed oracle (occurrence count, `not in X` membership, expected-value set) is a claim about the artifact, not the artifact. For SCPD-1 propagation on a multi-occurrence anchor, run the per-line occurrence count + the frozen-vs-live discriminator BEFORE writing the propagation pin, and do not apply a Critic's count/`not in` remedy as ACCEPTED-FIXED until it is mechanically recomputed. (slice-032/036 "reason about the claim, not the artifact" law → now extended to Critic-proposed oracles applied at TRI-1.)
- Frozen-history occurrences of a renamed anchor live on MULTIPLE surfaces (changelog entry + the edited shippability surface + test self-references). FBCD-1 sub-mode (a) full-fileset grep must enumerate ALL twins — especially on the surface the slice edits — not stop at the first hit. Candidate `/critic-calibrate` watch-list item.
- A SKILL.md-repointing slice must supersede/realign any `test_*_skill.py` mini-CAD prose-pin asserting the OLD prose in the SAME fix block — the SCPD-1 / mini-CAD consumer-reference-propagation class applied to prose-pins, not just shippability rows. (R-10; strongest standing follow-up candidate.)
- DR-1 dual-review keeps paying decisively on codification slices (N+1: slice-026/029/038/039) — M-add-1 prevented a guaranteed downstream false-FAIL on an already-ACCEPTED-FIXED finding. The structural blind spots remain backstopped by BC-PROJ-4 + the real-artifact runs, not new Critic dimensions.

## Slice 040 (realign-validate-slice-step-5-5-prose-pin) — 2026-05-18

### Worked
- Targeting the top open risk (R-10, score 3) the user named: the failing repro test pre-existed (`test_step4_5_5_consumes_machine_stable_command` failing slice-innocently on master since slice-038), so BFRD-1 was satisfied without `/repro` — risk-register-sourced conformance-bug precedent (slice-034 R-7 / slice-036 R-9). Surgical: ONE test function realigned, suite 659/1 → 660/0.
- Builder verification went deeper than the first Critic's M1 proposal: instead of re-heading the `### Fixed` changelog entry, grep proved the slice-036/R-9 conformance-fix precedent adds NO changelog entry at all → dropped the entry entirely. More precedent-faithful; META-1 stayed clean (parentless `###` would have polluted slice-039's v0.52.0 block).
- DR-1 meta-Critic was load-bearing again (N+1: 026/029/038/039/040): M-add-1 caught that MEPD-1 — the rule slice-039 minted one slice ago to govern exactly this no-changelog-entry decision — was never invoked by name, on the first slice it governs. Substance was already compliant; the discipline-citation was added.
- AC2 non-tautology proven by in-memory monkeypatch + restore-and-confirm (NOT `git checkout/restore/stash` — BC-PROJ-3/BC-GLOBAL-2 compliant by construction); proven independently at build and validate.

### Didn't work
- The first Critic + the Builder both reached the right no-changelog-entry substance without naming MEPD-1, the directly-governing rule minted in the immediately-prior slice (slice-039). Only DR-1 caught the missing discipline-citation. A freshly-minted rule is a first-Critic blind spot on the very next slice it governs — the prompt has not yet internalised it.
